# Compiler working notes

Record agreed design decisions here so later work does not repeat rejected
approaches. Keep the intended architecture distinct from what is implemented
and proved today.

## Current autonomous proof-pipeline policy (October 8)

[Proof-pipeline Decision authority](/home/andrew-peterson/.codex/skills/proof-pipeline/SKILL.md)
is the operational source of truth. Do not combine it with superseded packet rules.

- Authors own edits and repairs; checkers coordinate bounded checks directly with
  them. The coordinator owns interfaces, scheduling and in-scope redesign.
- The 150-line proof, 50-line fixture and 1,000-line tooling caps are retired.
  Size targets prompt simplification, not permission requests.
- Each command and complete fresh acceptance check stays under 120 seconds,
  including setup, diagnostics and descendant cleanup. No splitting to evade it.
- Keep independent review, complete claims, standard trust and exact implementation
  correspondence. Routine revisions reuse the approved contract; changing its
  behavior, domain or trust requires contract review.
- Repeated substantive rejections or measured bookkeeping failures trigger an
  in-scope redesign. There is no fixed retry allowance; evidence repairs do not
  erase unresolved findings.
- A delivered or explicitly handed-off phase releases its reservation after
  confirmed cleanup; the named successor proceeds. Authors restart collaborators
  with a turn-starting follow-up and wake the consumer of a finished dependency.
- One author owns the assembled result through a review-ready candidate. A
  passing helper does not finish that assignment. Repeated development failures
  at the same bookkeeping boundary prompt an in-scope redesign, without waiting
  for an acceptance rejection or user permission.
- Continue to the next unclosed obligation after acceptance. Ask the user only
  for necessary changes to outcome, language, trust, external authority or a
  user-set hard limit.

The remaining numeric rules are the 120-second execution/connected-check ceiling,
the requested four-agent team and engineering size targets. Team scheduling and
worker termination headroom are not additional approval gates. Do not add new
line, fixture, tooling or retry quotas. Authors/checkers handle ordinary diagnostics
directly; root handles connected progress, interfaces and repeated bottlenecks.
Missing acceptance evidence prevents integration, not further in-scope work.

trapped or exited result retains the input cell IDs in order, appending exactly
the consecutive IDs between the old and new allocation frontiers. Mutations
may change cell values; scope restoration does not reclaim their storage.
Fuel exhaustion exposes no state. The proof composes the actual native writers,
view synchronization loops and modeled host adapters in one mutual induction.
It assumes neither a successful execution nor a well-formed input.

`CellInventory` is a proof-library projection of the existing semantic state,
not a new compiler IR, emitted certificate or second proof tree. Its extension
law implies a nondecreasing frontier and retained cell lookup. It also preserves
`CellsWellFormed`, and preserves unique IDs when the input additionally has
unique IDs. Here `CellsWellFormed` means only that stored IDs are below the
frontier; it does not assert valid local references, value typing or heap safety.
Bad states are not claimed to be repaired.

`Evaluates`, `Executes`, `RunsExpr` and `RunsStmt` now expose the inventory law
and cell well-formedness preservation. A caller can use
`run.cellsWellFormed formedBefore` instead of proving a separate allocation
frame. Value and memory-frame obligations remain separate. Focused fixtures
exercise mutation, both loop evaluators, temporary slice storage, native view
registration, recursive parameter allocation, traps, nested host exit, gaps in
an initial inventory, invalid frontiers and duplicate initial IDs.

The allocation foundation is 398 lines: 123 for the inventory algebra and
275 for helper contracts, directed proof construction, mutual induction and
public consequences. The 66-line shared invariant driver now serves both
allocation and lexical preservation; moving it reduces the lexical module
from 269 to 220 lines, a net 17-line shared-infrastructure increase. Contract
integration adds 13 lines, and separate fixtures add 130: 428 lines of net
production-proof growth and 558 including tests. These are shared laws over
roughly 900 lines of evaluator/stateful-helper implementation, not per-function
proof expansions. No Lanius implementation or emission format changes.

Fresh full-source checking takes 15.09 s with built imports. The profiled
check takes 14.83 s and reports 0.207 s of type checking and 0.534 s of imports;
most measured work is proof construction/simplification. Cumulative profiler
labels overlap and are not an exclusive time partition. Broad elimination
search hit the default heartbeat limit while composing state steps. Directed
backward chaining of proved steps replaces that search; no limit was raised.
Axiom audits show only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`.
The final allocation, lexical and fuel fixtures plus ten existing proof-client
regression modules pass together in 29.01 s, including the allocation and
contract-client rebuilds with other shared imports already built.

The prior compiler cache gate remains stale and was not rerun for this change.
This is allocation metatheory and execution-contract integration, not a new
extractor/compiler correctness checkpoint. Type safety, general termination,
compiler simulation and the explicit trusted-output mechanism remain open.

Evidence: `target/verified-compiler/binary/allocation-fresh.log`,
`allocation-profile.log`, `allocation-tests.log` and `allocation-clients.log`.

## October 3: lexical bindings are preserved by the whole evaluator

`Semantics/LexicalFrame.lean` proves that every observed result of all seven
evaluator entry points retains the input state's lexical bindings, on normal
completion, traps and modeled host exits. This holds for arbitrary programs
and initial states; typing and well-formedness are not premises. The actual
native place writers, array-view synchronization loops, slice/string mappings
and host-result adapters are proved at their own boundaries and composed by
one mutual evaluator induction. A private proof-producing tactic instantiates
proved contracts and splits the actual definitions; it adds no trust premise.

This is binding preservation, not value or memory preservation. Assignment
can change a caller cell. Scope exit retains allocated cells, so a returned
reference remains backed by storage after its local name disappears. Those
properties are exercised by focused fixtures, alongside shadowing, matches,
returns, break/continue, uninitialized reads, traps, host exit and malformed
initial bindings. Fuel exhaustion has no observed state.

`RunsExpr.ofEval` and `RunsStmt.ofExec` now require only the finite completed
execution. Fuel independence supplies stability; this new theorem supplies
the lexical frame. `Evaluates.runs` and `Executes.runs` expose the same bridge
with fuel hidden. The internal caller is updated directly; no old-signature
compatibility wrapper was added. Existing contracts still retain their frame
facts for downstream use. Preservation of values, heap fragments, cell
well-formedness and typing are separate obligations, not consequences of this
binding theorem.

The shared foundation is 269 lines against roughly 900 lines of evaluator and
stateful-helper implementation. Contract integration adds 12 lines; focused
fixtures add 95 separate lines and update one existing call: 376 lines of
total proof/test growth. No Lanius source or emitted program format changes.
The fresh unprofiled check takes 12.89 s with built imports. The profiled
check takes 12.49 s: it reports 0.213 s of type checking and 0.518 s
of imports, with most measured work in tactics/simplification. Cumulative
profiler labels overlap and must not be summed as an exclusive partition.
The attempted reflexivity/assumption fast path showed no clear speedup and was
removed. The new fixtures, prior fuel fixtures and ten existing proof-client
modules pass together in 29.53 s, including the fresh foundation rebuild.
Audits show only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`.

The broader compiler cache artifact was not rechecked after this shared-library
change; its previous source-provenance manifest is no longer current. These
checks establish the metatheory and its execution-contract clients, not a new
extractor/compiler correctness checkpoint. Type safety, general termination
and compiler simulation remain open.

Evidence: `target/verified-compiler/binary/lexical-fresh.log`, `lexical-profile.log`,
`lexical-connected.log`, `lexical-tests.log` and `lexical-clients.log`.

## October 3: completed executions are independent of fuel

`Semantics/Fuel.lean` proves fuel monotonicity for all seven mutually recursive
evaluator entry points, for every program, state and input. Once a run produces
a value/completion, trap or host exit, any larger budget produces exactly the
same outcome and full state. Only `outOfFuel` may refine to a different outcome.
The proof covers recursion, loops, mutable storage and modeled host effects;
it requires no typing or well-formedness premise. It uses one mutual induction
and a small proof-producing tactic, not a duplicate evaluator or an assumed
execution. The separate native-memory helper budgets are not varied by this
theorem.

Consequences include uniqueness of completed outcomes across different budgets,
uniqueness of `Evaluates`/`Executes` values and states, and exclusion of a trap
for an expression with a successful evaluation. `StableExpr.ofEval` and
`StableStmt.ofExec` turn one completed run into an all-larger-budgets contract.
At this checkpoint the `RunsExpr`/`RunsStmt` bridges still required unchanged
lexical locals explicitly; the subsequent lexical-frame result removes that
premise. Existing value/empty-enum/skip contracts and contract
agreement now use these results instead of separate fuel arguments.

The shared foundation is 222 lines; its focused fixtures are 99 separate lines.
Fresh checking of the full foundation takes 6.55 s with imported infrastructure
already built. Focused fixtures pass in 1.18 s with built imports (4.16 s when
rebuilding the contract bridges), and seven existing proof-client regression
modules pass in 13.96 s. Fixtures cover all seven entry points,
short-circuiting, traps, printing, recursive calls, host exit and scope/cell
restoration. Axiom audits report only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and
`Quot.sound`, with no `sorryAx`, native evaluation axiom or new trusted premise.

This is evaluator metatheory, not type safety, universal termination, a proof
of the physical host runtime, or full extractor/compiler correctness. It lets
later software proofs establish one finite execution and reuse the generic
fuel result, rather than proving stability afresh for each function.

At this checkpoint the connected compiler cache gate passed: 82.32 s for the fresh proof and
fixture bundle, 90.34 s including setup and 4.04 s self-emission. It reuses
checked shared imports and admission of the byte-identical self-program; input
provenance was validated then. The first run hit the 105 s stop while also rebuilding
25 shared modules. The retry checked the entire artifact, not separate pieces.
These are integration timings, not a compiler checking-speed improvement.

Evidence: `target/verified-compiler/binary/fuel-fresh.log`, `fuel-stable.log`,
`fuel-tests.log`, `fuel-clients.log` and `cache-metatheory-current.log`.

## October 2: cache classification is not full literal validity

The old `Source.Meaning` demanded an `ExprLowers` witness at every cached node.
That is too strong for the actual inference phase. It assigns I32 from a
literal's tag before decoding its text or checking its range. In particular,
`2147483648` cannot lower as positive I32, but its parent `-2147483648` can lower
through the existing signed-minimum rule. These facts now have kernel-checked
regressions in `SurfaceElaboration/ClassificationTests.lean`.

`Source.Meaning` now carries `ExprClassifies`: type classification using the
existing source AST, ground types and operator typing rules. It does not claim
that malformed text or an out-of-range value is valid. The literal-lowering
bridge requires the independent value-validation proof. This corrects an
over-strong intermediate obligation; full source-to-IR semantic preservation
is still required downstream. Do not read the older `ExprLowers` cache entries
below as the current invariant. No generated typing tree, runtime checker or
new trust assumption was added.

`Updates.preserves` handles any finite sequence of justified native cache
stores. It retains table soundness while recursive queries select different
nodes and budgets in the same storage. Updates compose and preserve parent
slot writability. The real cold unary/literal query now derives both source
classifications and the sound resulting table via `actualColdPreserves`.
Actual arbitrary recursive queries, binding-scope changes, canonical nominal
handle interpretation and native/source correspondence still need proofs.

The connected cache gate passes in 34.45 s (43.31 s total), including all 390
runtime cases. Source composition rechecks across admitted dependencies in
1.25 s; the classification/value boundary regressions check in 0.65 s. Existing
Lanius sources and self-program admission are reused. The full lowering/producer
gates were not rerun. All audited theorems use only standard logical axioms.
The focused bundle is 1,518 lines (+121); the shared classification/bridge adds
30, for +151 proof/infrastructure lines plus 42 separate regression lines.

## October 2: the recursive lowerer owns inference validation

`body_expression.expression` must reject a failed type inference before calling
`expression_untyped`. The previous implementation could infer Unsupported for
`-true`, emit both nodes anyway, and return success without a root annotation.
Outer surface callers normally prevented this; internal recursive callers
should not depend on a duplicate outer check. Validation now lives at the
shared entry point, and the surface wrapper delegates to it. An exhausted
depth budget rejects before source-node access.

The new general theorem covers root/depth rejection and caller/memory framing,
not arbitrary recursive typing or preservation. It is 34 lines including
representation helpers; the error contracts are reused, not copied. Sixty
Core-evaluator cases cover cold/warm caches, valid and invalid Boolean
operations, annotations and rejection before IR access. The tests detect the
old implementation. Normal extraction/x86 reject the invalid source fixtures;
the nested Boolean/mutation example returns 37 in both. Choice still returns
7, with a fresh Lean termination/frame proof and a native execution test.

The previous full producer/printer connection was checked against the older
`decimal` embedding and is not a fresh check of this one. Cache provenance must

`target/verified-compiler/expression`; see `VERIFICATION.md` for commands.

Next proof boundary: relate the real inference cache and recursive lowering
to source-expression semantics and the existing IR-tree invariant. Do not
replace that with more unrelated writer proofs, or treat the new guard theorem
as completion of recursive lowering. The goal remains active and incomplete.

## 2026-09-27: Trust the verified extractor and Lanius typechecker

**Prove the tools correct once; do not reconstruct their typing and extraction
guarantees for every program they emit.**

Lanius's typechecker owns Lanius typing checks. For example, it checks that a
function's arguments match its parameter types. The normal Lean extraction
path must not repeat that check through per-call typing proofs or dependent
type inference.

The intended arrangement is:

1. Prove the Lanius typechecker sound and the extractor meaning-preserving,
   connecting those proofs to the actual implementations.
2. Use the shared typed IR for normal x86 compilation and Lean extraction.
3. Emit readable Lean representing the program directly, not an integer
   certificate or a program-sized typing derivation.
4. Rely on the verified tools for typing and extraction guarantees. Write
   subsequent proofs about the extracted program's behavior.

A typed IR inside the compiler does not require emitted Lean syntax to carry
scope, signature, result-type, and proof indices at every node. An extracted
call should contain its callee and arguments, not evidence that Lanius already
checked their types. Lean still checks that the emitted definitions are valid
Lean; that should not reconstruct Lanius's typechecking.

### Do not repeat the rejected approach

Removing a separate typing-proof tree is not enough if the same obligations
move into an intrinsically typed program tree. The previous representation did
this, and its fresh self-module check took 87.27 seconds. That was not the
intended end state. Before optimizing another lookup, typeclass search, or
per-node proof, ask whether the verified extractor makes that work unnecessary.

The performance target is seconds for fresh per-program extraction and
admission into Lean. Checking the tools' general correctness proofs is a
separate cost. Cached checks of the same artifact do not meet the fresh-check
target, and trusting extraction does not prove arbitrary program properties.

### Trust boundary and remaining work

The general extractor/compiler correctness proof is not finished. Trusting the
current executable's output would therefore be provisional, not a completed
verification result.

The user accepts an explicit trusted-output boundary. Its exact mechanism is
still to be chosen: a theorem about the extractor does not automatically tell
Lean's kernel that an external file came from that verified executable. State
the execution and import assumptions, connect them to the exact input/output,
and keep them visible. Do not silently add axioms or report provisional trust
as a kernel-checked derivation.

This decision supersedes older plans that require the normal extraction path
to reauthenticate every program's Lanius typing facts. Those plans may describe
existing code or bootstrap validation, but they are not the target architecture.

## Implementation checkpoint: proof-free output

The normal emitter now produces ordinary Core syntax through readable Surface
notation, with no per-node typing evidence, signature lookup proofs, or
automatic program-typing theorem. Calls do not check argument types in Lean.
Phantom type labels remain only to support source names and notation.
No new axioms were introduced.

The fresh full self-module check fell to 38.38 seconds (996 functions and 671
constants); emission took 18.89 seconds. This is progress, not the seconds-scale
target. Twelve fixtures execute in Lean and x86, ten pass the independent
source-agreement checker, and malformed source fails at the Lanius frontend.
Moving return-path validation into shared Lanius IR checks also fixed acceptance
of non-unit functions that could fall through. The two raw-slice fixtures remain
outside the independent source checker's fragment. See
[VERIFICATION.md](VERIFICATION.md) for measured coverage.

Next: reduce the remaining ordinary elaboration/emission cost without
reintroducing typing reconstruction, and finish the general correctness proofs
and explicit trusted-output boundary. Do not mistake the optional independent
typing audit for that proof.

## Elaboration follow-up

Function references now retain only their result label and ID. Parameter types
remain on function declarations; copying them into references served no purpose
after removing per-call typing checks.

The declaration trace attributes 23.93 seconds to elaborating 996 function
bodies and 3.54 seconds to the other 3,895 definition bodies. Tracing adds
overhead, so these totals are diagnostic, not an end-user timing.

Removing redundant reference metadata passed the twelve-program Lean/x86 gate,
ten source-agreement audits, and ten malformed-source cases. A fresh self check
still took about 43 seconds. Experiments that inlined binding descriptions,
specialized reference elaboration, or directly constructed local IDs measured
37.09, 37.76, and 38.02 seconds. None establishes a useful improvement over the
38.38-second checkpoint, so those notation experiments were removed. Do not
report this work as another large performance gain.

The final retained version checked freshly, including artifact writing, in
35.58 seconds. Its profile reports 20.2 seconds of elaboration and 1.18 seconds
of kernel checking. The twelve-program gate passed again in 13.91 seconds.
These are incremental results, not the few-second self-check target.

The next substantial optimization must reduce ordinary elaboration of the
readable function bodies while preserving their exact Core meaning. Do not
resume typing-proof reconstruction, add a second emitted representation, or
count cached program checks as fresh-check improvements.

## Direct Core construction

The shared function notation now constructs Core nodes directly instead of
expanding a body into nested polymorphic builder applications. Labels resolve
names while reading the notation; they are not stored as a second tree or
checked as Lanius typing judgments. Functions retain their source name and one
Core declaration. The emitted source remains readable Lean.

The fixed-program comparison preserved every Core declaration byte-for-byte,
including all 996 functions and 671 constants. A fresh run of the rebuilt
extractor on its current sources took 17.57 seconds to emit, 21.65 seconds to
check and write the Lean module, and 453 ms for the optional typing audit.
The full diagnostic script, including source-closure setup, took 45.23 seconds.
The generated program definition has no axiom dependencies; this is a fact
about its data definition, not a compiler-correctness theorem.

Elaboration now reports 7.53 seconds, versus 20.2 before this change. Kernel
checking reports 1.09 seconds. Lean compilation and artifact writing remain
material costs. Enabling closed-subexpression extraction made the fixed check
slower (28.80 seconds), so that option remains disabled. Eagerly normalizing
parameter metadata also added work and was removed.

The twelve-program Lean/x86 gate, ten source-agreement audits, malformed-source
rejection, captured-variable/scope tests, and runtime-import mutation gate pass.
Name-resolution tests ensure a function named `Builtin.toI32` is not mistaken
for the intrinsic. The seconds-scale target and general tool-correctness proofs
remain open.

## Native generation: constant resolution

Profiling the full extractor closure found that printing Lean took only about
0.25 seconds. The first phase trace measured 6.77 seconds for loading/frontend
work, 2.26 seconds for catalog preparation, 0.23 seconds for signatures, and
8.59 seconds for body lowering. A separate sampling run attributed 39% of its
samples, including callees, to `resolve_constant_path`.

That resolver repeatedly visited unrelated modules and searched imports even
when a local value already shadowed them. It now filters qualified paths once
per owner group and searches local values before imports. Local functions and
enum variants still shadow imported constants; matching module names do not
bypass imports or privacy. This changes shared lowering, not the Lean notation,
typechecker policy, or output format. The production change adds 21 net lines.

On the same frozen 80-unit input, uninstrumented generation fell from 17.24 to
11.70 seconds. The entire emitted Lean file was byte-identical. The fresh run
on the updated sources then measured 12.25 seconds for generation, 20.89 seconds
for Lean checking/artifact writing, and 39.05 seconds for the diagnostic script
including setup and the optional 453 ms typing audit. It still contains 996
functions and 671 constants. Logs are under `target/verified-compiler/`:
`resolution-comparison.log` and `resolution-self.log`.

The twelve-program Lean/x86 gate and ten source-agreement audits pass. Constant
dependency checks cover forward/shared/imported references, local shadowing,
cycles, and seven changed-value mutations. The visibility gate now requires
clean rejection in both extraction modes and x86, including private constants,
missing imports, wrong module prefixes, and function/variant shadowing.

The next native performance target is parsing. Lean admission is still the
larger end-to-end cost. The few-second target, general correctness proofs, and
explicit trusted-output boundary remain unfinished.

## Native generation: parser state lookup

The parser now caches each state's next grammar symbol or completed
nonterminal. It also uses sixteen lookup buckets per chart to find duplicate
states. Collisions compare the complete state key. A separate chart queue
preserves insertion order and the first retained derivation; the index changes
lookup, not the grammar, parse order, or emitted representation.

The cost is larger scratch storage: states grow from 72 to 96 bytes and each
chart gains 64 bytes of bucket heads. The shared default workspace grows from
64 to 96 MiB to preserve at least the previous state capacity throughout the
supported token range. The extractor now allocates that shared capacity rather
than deriving its parser allocation from the output-buffer size.

The rebuilt production parser passes all 511 binary token streams through
length eight against the balanced-language reference, as well as nullable,
split-token, duplicate-rule, ambiguous-derivation, collision, and storage-boundary
cases. The twelve-program Lean/x86 gate and ten exact-source audits also pass;
malformed programs still fail cleanly at the Lanius boundary.
Native syntax-tree materialization and the source-to-syntax composition test
also pass. The latter's split-token case needed 132 workspace words under the
new layout, exceeding its old 128-word test buffer; that internal buffer is now
256 words. Dedicated exhaustion and canary tests retain exact boundary checks.

On identical frozen input, generation took 11.62 seconds before and 10.53
seconds after, with byte-identical Lean output. A fresh run on current sources
took **9.40 seconds to generate**, **19.92 seconds to check/write**, and
**35.21 seconds overall**, including setup and the optional 433 ms typing audit.
The self-module contains 997 functions and 673 constants. These are separate
measurements; use the frozen-input pair for the parser's isolated speedup.
Logs: `parser-final-comparison.log`, `parser-emission.log`, and `parser-self.log`
under `target/verified-compiler/`.

Two experiment notes matter for later work. Skipping repeated prediction groups
did not reduce the measured instruction count, so that shortcut was removed.
Also, one bootstrap build overwrote a tagged-enum payload when a state-link
field followed it. Keeping link fields before the payload-bearing action fixed
the focused regression; the underlying GPU compiler layout issue has not been
diagnosed or fixed. Do not describe this as a general aggregate-layout fix.

Lean admission remains the larger cost. The current overlapping profile reports
6.73 seconds of elaboration, 1.80 seconds of typeclass inference, 1.04 seconds of
kernel checking, about 6.7 seconds of compilation, and 4.07 seconds of artifact
writing. The few-second target and the general correctness/import contracts
remain open. Parser tests and self-extraction are regression evidence, not a
proof of the optimized parser implementation.

Inspection of the installed Lean compiler confirmed that postponing compilation
defers it to a later compilation/execution step. It was not enabled or counted
as a speedup. No custom compiler passes, noncomputable replacements, or kernel
bypasses were added. The next Lean-side work should reduce the cost of admitting
the same executable definitions, not omit or defer a required phase.

## 2026-09-28: Direct parameter binders

Defined functions no longer emit one Lean `Var` declaration per parameter and
then assemble those values into a dependent `Vars` list. The notation reads the
function's existing signature labels and binds source names directly to their
resolved IDs:

```lean
def choose : Function [Bool, I32] I32 :=
  function (id := 4, name := "choose",
      arguments := [flag := 31, value := 72], bindings := []) do
    if flag then
      return value
    else
      return 0
```

Those binders populate the notation's local-name context and the ordinary Core
parameter list. They introduce no typing evidence. Captured Lean values and
computed signatures remain supported. The Lanius printer and in-repo examples
use the new syntax directly; the old printing helper was deleted, not retained
behind a compatibility branch. Runtime-import declarations still use the small
existing `Function.external` helper.

On the same frozen program, the new output removed 2,524 lines and 347,950 bytes
of parameter scaffolding. The complete evaluated Core snapshot remained
byte-identical: 7,308,849 bytes covering all 997 functions and 673 constants.
The fresh module check took 17.89 seconds, compared with the previous
19.92-second check. A fresh extraction of the updated sources then took
**9.77 seconds to emit**, **18.29 seconds to check/write**, and **33.87 seconds
overall**, including setup and the optional 432 ms typing audit. It contains
996 functions and 673 constants; the missing function is the deleted printer
helper, not omitted extraction coverage.

The focused notation tests cover parameter order/types, captured IDs, generic
and computed signatures, shadowing, mutation, records, constructors, and
intrinsics. The twelve-program Lean/x86 gate and ten exact-source audits pass;
malformed programs remain rejected. The generated program definition has no
axiom dependencies. This is regression evidence, not the unfinished general
extractor/compiler correctness proof.

Logs under `target/verified-compiler/`: `direct-parameters-fixed.log`,
`direct-parameters-self.log`, `direct-parameters-emission.log`, and
`direct-parameters-scope.log`. The fixed-program snapshots are
`parameters-before.core` and `parameters-after.core`.

### Measurements that did not justify a production change

- Marking the 1,670 function/constant reference declarations `macro_inline`
  checked in 19.99 seconds: no useful wall-clock improvement.
- Loading a freshly built native notation plugin reduced reported
  interpretation to milliseconds, but the whole check still took 19.40 seconds.
  The shared native build took 3.18 seconds. No plugin-loading wrapper was added.
- One worker took 28.62 seconds; synchronous elaboration took 25.07 seconds.
  Normal parallel checking remains enabled.
- Detailed instrumentation measured only 129 ms for Core type-label resolution,
  116 ms for field lookup, and 25.6 ms for constructor lookup. A field cache is
  not the next substantial performance opportunity. Temporary instrumentation
  was removed.
- CPU sampling still did not recover useful caller stacks for this Lean build.
  Its allocation and object-compaction samples are not a caller-level diagnosis.

The latest overlapping profile reports 6.06 seconds of elaboration, 1.47 seconds
of typeclass inference, 926 ms of kernel checking, about 6.1 seconds of Lean
compilation, and 3.61 seconds of artifact writing. The remaining cost is spread
across declaration elaboration, compilation, and serialization. The few-second
target and the general correctness/import contracts remain open.

## 2026-09-28: Reuse module declaration spans

Constant-path resolution now searches the owning module's recorded declaration
range rather than scanning every allocation to find that module's rows. The
shared pipeline already checks that these ranges partition the global table.
Lookup checks each selected range's bounds and row ownership, filters exact
module paths and imports once per owner, and retains per-declaration privacy,
local shadowing, and ambiguity checks. It adds no index or output format and
removes seven net Lanius lines relative to the preceding implementation.

On identical 80-unit input, the final comparison measured **11.58 seconds before
and 9.38 seconds after**, with byte-identical Lean output. An earlier comparison
measured 10.41 versus 8.65 seconds. These pairs establish the native lookup gain;
they do not establish a Lean-checking speedup.

Fourteen programs now execute with the expected results in Lean and x86, and
twelve pass independent source-agreement checks. New cases distinguish qualified
constants with the same spelling and ignore an unimported module's same-named
constant. Ambiguous imported constants fail in both source-unit orders and all
three output modes. Existing privacy, function/variant shadowing, constant
mutation, dependency, and cycle regressions also pass.

The fresh current-source run passed with 996 functions and 673 constants:
9.76 seconds to emit, 23.21 seconds to check/write, and 39.97 seconds for the
complete diagnostic script. The optional typing audit took 468 ms. The higher
Lean time also affects the unchanged previous program: subsequent fresh checks
in the same session took 24.71 seconds for the previous program and 24.22 for
the current one. No Lean admission speedup is claimed. The few-second target
remains open. Logs are `owner-spans-final-comparison.log`, `owner-spans-self.log`,
`owner-spans-emission.log`, `owner-spans-visibility.log`, and
`owner-spans-constants.log` under `target/verified-compiler/`; the paired Lean
checks are `owner-spans-lean-before.log` and `owner-spans-lean-after.log`.

Experiments not retained:

- Inlining typeclass metadata checked in 18.80 seconds. Shared non-inlined
  literal/return constructors checked in 18.31 seconds. Neither improved the
  preceding 18.29-second whole-module check, so both were removed.
- Extending owner-span lookup to nominal types triggered a GPU-bootstrap
  rejection in the combined build, although the file parsed alone. That
  extension was removed; the retained constant-only change builds successfully.
  The cause of the combined-build failure is not established.

A separate diagnostic tried compiling the extractor with the Lanius-written
x86 compiler. Both current sources and the unchanged pre-edit snapshot returned
`BackendFailed` (exit 11), without producing an executable. This is an existing
self-hosting gap, distinct from successful GPU bootstrapping and self-extraction.
The later "Compile the extractor through Lanius" checkpoint below diagnoses
and closes this executable gap. General compiler correctness remains unproved.

## 2026-09-28: Native declarations own field and constructor names

The notation now reads constructor ordinals, payload labels, and record-field
positions from the emitted Lean inductive declarations. Record constructor
binders retain their source field names instead of `field0`, `field1`, etc.
This removes the per-field `Field` and per-variant `Constructor` instances,
their APIs, and four obsolete Lanius printer functions. Internal callers were
migrated directly; there is no fallback metadata table. The per-type Core ID
instances remain. This is name resolution, not Lanius typechecking: it adds no
argument-type proofs or second program tree.

On the same frozen 80-unit source closure, output lost 1,395 duplicate instance
declarations, 3,301 lines, and 256,546 bytes. The complete evaluated Core
snapshots are byte-identical (7,286,004 bytes), including every type, constant,
function, and the entrypoint. Fresh Lean admission, including artifact writing,
took **22.34 seconds before and 19.33 seconds after**. Shared infrastructure
was already built; neither measurement reused checked program declarations.
The production Lanius printer is 87 lines shorter across the two changed files.

Fresh self-extraction of the updated implementation passed with 992 functions
and 673 constants: **7.75 seconds to emit, 19.21 seconds to check/write, and
33.50 seconds for the complete diagnostic script**. The four removed functions
are the obsolete metadata printers, not omitted extraction coverage. The
optional typing audit took 341 ms, and the generated program definition has
no axiom dependencies. Do not attribute the generation-time difference to
this change; the controlled comparison establishes the Lean-side result.

Fourteen Lean/x86 execution fixtures, twelve independent exact-source audits,
same-typed field/initializer mutation checks, malformed-source rejection, and
public namespaces pass. Focused notation tests cover private owners, identical
constructor/field spellings under different owners, applied generic types,
aliases, reserved names, and mutually recursive labels. Reading declaration
metadata does not establish that the extractor itself preserves semantics;
the general correctness proofs and explicit execution/import boundary remain
open, as does the few-second fresh-admission target.

Public constants (including aggregate payloads), seven changed-value mutations,
and invalid-constant rejection also pass, as does the native semantic-IR output

## 2026-09-28: Link native nominal declarations directly

Normal and checked Lean output now use `link (types := [...], constants := ...,
functions := ...)`. It obtains nominal fields and variants from the native Lean
declarations, with their existing Core IDs. All types remain explicit, including
unused types; each Core declaration list retains its original order. The old
Core type-table printers and naming helper were deleted. Constants and function
bodies are transferred unchanged, without typechecking them again.

The shared label-reading code moved into `Surface/Labels.lean` for body notation
and linking to use together. Production Lanius shrank by 169 lines; shared Lean
grew by 50 net lines, with 48 lines of focused tests. On identical frozen input,
the actual printer emits 829 fewer lines and its complete evaluated Core is
byte-identical to the previous emitter's 7,286,004-byte snapshot. Fourteen
Lean/x86 fixtures, twelve source-agreement checks, malformed-source rejection,
public namespaces, and the native semantic-IR test pass. Focused tests cover
unused types, mixed declaration order, nonconsecutive IDs, generic fields,
empty records/programs, and ordinary Lean record updates. The latter caught and
fixed an accidental keyword collision in the first notation prototype.

This is an architecture simplification, not an established speedup. A
same-module-name comparison took 18.69 seconds before and 18.10 after, but
earlier differently named modules took about 18.5 versus 20.0–20.1 seconds.
Current-source self-extraction takes **8.47 seconds to emit, 19.25 seconds to
check/write, and 34.27 seconds overall**. It contains 989 functions and 673
constants; three obsolete printer functions disappeared. The optional typing
audit takes 424 ms and the generated definition has no axiom dependencies.
The few-second target and general correctness/import proofs remain open.

The diagnostic command profile places function-list assembly at about 0.6
seconds and the final record at milliseconds; it is not the main cost.
Disabling Lean's reuse optimization took 19.38 seconds versus 18.56 normally,
so that setting was not retained. Ordinary function elaboration/compilation
and artifact writing remain the main admission costs.

Logs: `native-link-emission.log`, `native-link-public.log`,
`native-link-self.log`, and `native-link-same-module-{before,after}.log` under
`target/verified-compiler/`. The actual printer comparison is
`native-names-after.core` versus `native-link-printed.core`.

## 2026-09-28: Index parser completion searches

A fresh native profile measured 3.30 seconds inside the parser and only
0.074 seconds in lexing/token preparation across the 80-unit self-input.
Completion and nullable replay scanned whole charts for matching actions.
They now use ordered bucket queues keyed by nonterminal and action kind.
Each queue is a stable subsequence of its chart: exact action comparisons
handle collisions, insertion order preserves the first derivation, and a scan
sees matching states appended while it runs. Prediction and grammar semantics
are unchanged.

The index adds eight bytes per state and 128 bytes per lattice chart. Shared
parser scratch grows from 96 to 120 MiB to preserve the previous state capacity
throughout the supported 0–65,536-token range. At the upper bound it holds
947,749 states, versus 939,348 before. Separate integer arrays hold queue heads
and tails; there is no packed head/tail record format. The parser adds 56 production
Lanius lines; it adds no Lean proof code or new trust assumptions.

On identical frozen input, hardware counters measured **60.76 billion versus
51.55 billion user instructions** (15.2% fewer), with wall time **7.77 versus
6.70 seconds**. The entire 2,373,151-byte Lean output was identical. Other wall
times varied; the instruction count confirms less work independent of that
variation. The fresh current-source run contains 990 functions and 674
constants and takes **7.51 seconds to emit, 14.37 seconds to check/write, and
27.42 seconds overall**. The optional typing audit takes 337 ms.
No Lean-side optimization was retained in this step, so do
not attribute its lower check time to the parser. General correctness proofs,
the explicit execution/import boundary, and the few-second target remain open.

Focused checks cover all 511 short balanced-grammar token streams, all 15
short streams for colliding nullable nonterminals, exact retained derivations,
ambiguity order, split tokens, storage exhaustion, and canaries. Disabling the
exact nullable-action comparison in an isolated mutant fails the derivation
test (exit 52). Native syntax materialization and source-to-syntax composition
pass. Rebuilt extractor and compiler binaries pass fourteen Lean/x86 fixtures,
twelve independent source-agreement audits, and malformed-source rejection.
These are regression checks, not a proof of the optimized parser.

Logs: `actions-{before,after}-perf.log`, `actions-final-build.log`,
`actions-connected.log`, and `actions-self.log` under `target/verified-compiler/`.
`ActionsBefore.lean` and `ActionsAfter.lean` are the fixed-input comparison.
The validated binaries are `extractor-direct` and `compiler-direct`; their
predecessors are retained as `*-before-actions`.

Lean experiments not retained: a constructor-only compiler-pass prototype
checked the full module in 20.11 seconds versus 21.13 normally, too small a
gain to justify a custom compilation path. Native Lean module export filtering
cannot be tried by changing only the generated header: Lean rejects importing
the current non-module shared library from a module. Neither experiment changed

The first action-index prototype interleaved heads and tails in an integer
array; the retained version uses separate arrays. Wall-time comparisons alone

## 2026-09-28: Compile the extractor through Lanius

The Lanius-written x86 compiler now produces the complete extractor executable,
and that executable self-extracts successfully. The 3,054,768-byte ELF emits
exactly the same 2,354,278-byte Lean module as the GPU-built extractor on the
same 80 source units. This closes a functional gap, not a correctness theorem.
The compiler producing this ELF is still bootstrapped with the GPU compiler.

Four failures had prevented this path from working:

- Workspace sizing summed every function's events against the old 65,536-word
  wire limit. Native sizing now follows storage lifetimes: per-function locals
  and break patches reuse space; function positions and call fixups accumulate.
  The full extractor needs 4,279 rows. The maximum row limit stays unchanged.
- Shared lowering emitted `usize` arithmetic for allocation sizes, but x86 only
  supported unsigned equality. One width-aware emitter now handles signed i32
  and unsigned usize binary operations, with wrapping arithmetic, unsigned
  comparisons/division, logical right shift, and explicit invalid-shift traps.
- Literal-string compilation crashed while converting an event's typed slice
  to a pointer and back. Passing the existing slice directly removes that
  conversion and fixes the small reproducer and full extractor compilation.
- The final image exceeded the fixed 2 MiB output cap. The cap is now 4 MiB;
  byte-as-i32 output scratch increases from 8 to 16 MiB. This remains a bounded
  image allocation, not a growable buffer or a new semantic guarantee.

Sixteen programs pass Lean/x86 execution using the Lanius-built extractor;
fourteen also pass independent exact-source checking. Four unsigned-arithmetic
trap cases cover shift counts 64 and 2^32, division by zero, and remainder by
zero in both Lean and native execution. Malformed-source rejection still passes.
The existing string oracle needed a binder rename because `function` is now a
notation keyword; its claim was unchanged.

`backend_native_workspace_check.sh` compiles its regression with the
Lanius-written compiler, then runs both the regression and its emitted ELF.
It covers 65,792 events across small functions, 496 accumulated call fixups
including string helpers, insufficient workspace, and a trailing canary. An
isolated mutant restoring the global event cap fails this test with exit 2.
The GPU compiler still rejects this fixture's dependency closure at an unchanged
`constant_index.lani` expression; the cause is not diagnosed. The retained test
does not depend on that GPU build path.

A fresh full Lean check of the native extractor's output passed with 990
functions and 674 constants: 18.05 seconds to emit, 21.26 seconds to check/write,
and 48.69 seconds for the complete diagnostic script. The optional typing audit
took 390 ms; the generated program definition has no axiom dependencies. The
Lean input is byte-identical to the earlier 14.37-second check, so the higher
Lean timing is not a representation change or additional checking obligation.

A subsequent sequential same-input comparison measured 7.22 seconds and
50.83 billion user instructions for the GPU-built extractor versus 15.62 seconds
and 88.08 billion instructions for the Lanius-built one. Code generation now
works on the full extractor but needs optimization; it is not a speedup.
`extractor-direct` therefore remains the faster GPU-built executable.
`extractor-native.elf` is the validated Lanius-built alternative. The fixed
compiler is installed as `compiler-direct`, with its predecessor retained as
`compiler-before-native-fixes`.

Logs are under `target/verified-compiler/`: `native-connected.log`,
`native-workspace.log`, `native-self-check.log`, and
`{bootstrap,native}-extractor-perf.log`. `BootstrapSelf.lean`, `NativeSelf.lean`,
and `SelfCore.lean` compare byte-for-byte. The few-second target, general
typechecker/extractor/backend proofs, and explicit execution/import boundary
remain open. Do not call this a verified native bootstrap or proof of the
compiler compiling itself.

## 2026-09-28: Resolve nominal types from one candidate catalog

Sampling the Lanius-built extractor attributed 31% of its time to nominal-type
lookup and its callees. Every type use rescanned all declarations, repeatedly
classifying functions, constants, modules, and imports that could not match.
Shared lowering now builds one typed catalog of structs, enums, and aliases.
Lookups retain the original name, module, visibility, shadowing, and ambiguity
rules. The catalog preserves candidate order and duplicates, and follows alias
targets in their defining module. It introduces no serialization or Lean proof
obligations. Its allocation is bounded by the original declaration-row count.

Sequential comparisons on the same frozen 80-unit input:

| Extractor executable | Before | After | User instructions before / after |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| GPU-built | 7.05 s | 5.67 s | 50.83 / 39.89 billion |
| Lanius-built x86 | 11.95 s | 8.42 s | 88.08 / 67.32 billion |

The complete Lean output is byte-identical before and after, and between both
executables. Instruction counts fall by 21.5% and 23.6%, respectively. These
pairs measure generation only, not a Lean checking speedup. An earlier attempt
that filtered module owners before names increased native work to 106.22
billion instructions; it was removed.

The fresh current-source run covers 991 functions and 674 constants: **5.49
seconds to emit, 15.68 seconds to check/write Lean, and 27.85 seconds for the
full diagnostic**, including source-closure setup and the optional 419 ms typing
audit. The new function builds the catalog. The Lean profile reports 4.44
seconds of elaboration, 915 ms of kernel checking, and 3.09 seconds of artifact
serialization; compiler categories overlap and must not be added as phases.

Seventeen Lean/x86 programs and fifteen independent source-agreement audits
pass, along with four arithmetic traps and malformed-source rejection. The
visibility gate covers local aliases shadowing several imports, qualified alias
chains, private types, ambiguity in both source orders, and cycles. Its catalog
regression covers 252 row-count/kind-order combinations, repeated names, stale
category counts, and invalid capacities. Removing alias candidates in an
isolated mutant makes that regression fail with exit 3. The test uses branches
for its numeric case generator: integer-pattern matching is outside the current
Lanius-written frontend's supported fragment.

Validated artifacts are `extractor-direct`, `compiler-direct`, and the alternative
`extractor-native.elf`. Logs are `nominal-catalog-{connected,visibility,self}.log`
and `nominal-catalog-{before,after,gpu-before,gpu-after}-perf.log`, under
`target/verified-compiler/`. No new Lean proof code or trust assumptions were
added. The seconds-scale admission target and general correctness/import
contracts remain open. Next, profile the remaining Lean admission cost; do not
confuse native-generation improvements with closing those proof obligations.

Lean follow-up: a 5 ms declaration trace confirms that time is spread across
compiling the emitted function data, rather than a single large typing proof or
link operation. Tracing itself took 19.36 seconds and adds overhead; use the
15.68-second ordinary check above for that checkpoint. The largest individual
compilation in the trace was about 95 ms, and linking elaborated in about 84 ms.

A shared, non-inlined i32 literal constructor preserved the complete evaluated
Core snapshot (7,219,695 bytes), but reduced fresh-check instructions only from
109.87 to 108.74 billion. It took 18.85 seconds versus 17.85 for the unchanged
notation in isolated runs. The experiment was removed and the original shared
notation rebuilt. No compiler passes were disabled, no compilation was deferred,
and no new production Lean definitions were retained. The first timing attempt
overlapped a shared-infrastructure build and was discarded; it is not a paired
performance result. Logs: `literal-{before,after}-{perf,check}.log` and
`nominal-catalog-lean-trace.log` under `target/verified-compiler/`.

## 2026-09-28: Copy only the projected value in x86

The Lanius x86 emitter no longer copies an entire record just to read one
field. An immediate field projection may use the record's address directly;
nested projections keep that address until the final field is read. This is
an emitter-local choice, not a new IR node, source reference type, or Lean
representation.

Aggregate values still need snapshots before later operands can mutate their
source. Projecting a record or slice as a value therefore copies that field,
not its enclosing record. Indexing still captures the slice descriptor before
evaluating the index, then reads the selected element after index effects.
Initialization and bounds checks remain in place. One shared helper handles
aggregate snapshots for locals, indexed values, and projected fields.

On identical frozen input, the Lanius-built extractor uses **47.55 billion user
instructions instead of 67.32 billion**, a 29.4% reduction. Sequential runs took
**6.93 versus 9.73 seconds**, with byte-identical complete Lean output. The
extractor ELF shrinks from 3,058,100 to 2,094,764 bytes. Other work was active on
the machine; instruction counts are the more stable comparison. This improves
the Lanius-built executable, not the GPU-built extractor or Lean admission.

The fresh native self-run covers the same 991 functions and 674 constants:
**7.50 seconds to emit, 17.05 seconds to check/write Lean, and 31.02 seconds for
the diagnostic**, including setup and the optional 357 ms typing audit. Shared
Lean infrastructure was already built; the complete generated module was
checked freshly in one invocation. The extractor closure does not include the
backend emitter, so the source program being extracted did not grow here.

Eighteen programs pass Lean/x86 execution, fifteen pass independent source
agreement, and four arithmetic traps plus an uninitialized field read agree.
The new projection fixture checks aggregate capture before a mutating argument,
scalar capture before a mutating right operand, index effects before loading
an element, descriptor capture before a mutating index, and projections from
local and returned records. Its kernel-checked execution test needs a local
recursion-depth limit of 4,096; production Lean options are unchanged. Two
isolated mutants fail as intended: omitting projected-record snapshots exits 2,
and omitting only slice snapshots exits 6. The native workspace regression also
passes. The heap fixture remains outside the independent source checker's
supported fragment; execution checks are not a general backend theorem.

Logs are `projection-{before,after}-perf.log`, `projection-connected.log`,
`projection-native-connected.log`, `projection-visibility.log`,
`projection-workspace.log`, and `projection-self.log` under
`target/verified-compiler/`. The updated compiler also compiles itself; that
Lanius-built compiler rebuilds the extractor ELF byte-for-byte identically.
This is an executable bootstrap check, not a verified-bootstrap theorem.
Validated binaries are `compiler-direct` and `extractor-native.elf`, with
their predecessors kept as `compiler-before-projection` and
`extractor-native-before-projection`. The faster GPU-built `extractor-direct`
is unchanged and remains the default. General tool-correctness proofs, the explicit
execution/import contract, and the few-second fresh-check target remain open.

### Lean module-format experiment: not retained

An isolated copy converted the nine required shared Lean modules and the whole
self-module to Lean's newer module format. All compiler phases ran, and the
evaluated Core snapshot matched the original exactly. Fresh checking used
108.61 billion instructions and took 20.18 seconds: essentially the same work
as the ordinary representation, not a useful improvement. The exported
`.olean` was smaller, but including the private, server, and IR artifacts gave
about 41.5 MB, versus about 40.3 MB before. No production files use this
experiment, and no compilation or kernel checking was omitted or deferred.
Logs: `module-probe-self.log` and `module-probe-perf.log`.

## 2026-09-28: Core-expression sharing experiment, not retained

The generated function definitions contain 754,389 Lean term nodes when counted
without sharing. An isolated notation experiment introduced ordinary Lean
`let` bindings for repeated, fully applied Core expression constructors. It
kept the readable input unchanged, ran every compiler phase and kernel check,
and preserved the complete evaluated Core snapshot exactly.

It did not justify a production change. Fresh-check instructions increased
from 109.90 to 119.03 billion (8.3%), and the artifact grew from 40,272,424 to
41,548,664 bytes. Wall times were 15.53 and 14.62 seconds, respectively, but that
small difference does not establish less work in the face of the counter and
size regressions. Production notation is unchanged. Logs and the isolated copy
are under `target/verified-compiler/sharing-*`; `SharingAfter.core` matches the
earlier complete Core snapshot. Repeated literal/constructor sharing is not
the next substantial Lean-admission opportunity.

## 2026-09-28: Reject duplicate callable declarations before emission

Shared lowering accepted two unused functions with the same name in one source
unit. The x86 path emitted an executable, while the extractor emitted duplicate
public Lean declarations that could not check. The GPU compiler rejected the
same source. The language description also forbids function-name shadowing;
checking only names reached by a function body missed unused duplicates.

The existing callable/variant name index now rejects a second callable with the
same owner and complete spelling during insertion. It covers defined functions,
external functions, and a defined/external collision in either order. It does
not reject distinct spellings that share a hash bucket, or variant names merely
because another enum uses the same spelling. Imports retain their existing
resolution rules. This is a unit-local declaration check, not a new claim of
bundle-wide namespace validation or complete frontend correctness.

The four new rejection fixtures all succeeded with the old extractor and x86
compiler; both now reject them cleanly. A positive fixture deliberately uses
two function names with equal coarse hash keys. The existing namespace fixture
also exercises same-spelled functions from different modules. No new Lean
proof machinery or emitted metadata is needed.

The fresh self-module contains 992 functions and 674 constants: **5.46 seconds
to emit, 15.88 seconds to check/write, and 28.09 seconds for the complete
diagnostic**, including setup and the optional 425 ms typing audit. The new
function is the shared callable-kind predicate. On identical frozen input,
generation instructions change from 39,894,616,245 to 39,894,891,656 (less than
0.001%); times were 6.18 and 6.09 seconds. The entire Lean output is unchanged
on that valid input. The Lanius-built extractor also emits current sources
byte-identically to the GPU-built executable.

Bootstrap caveat: an initial implementation repeated the enum-kind match
inside the insertion function. Its GPU-built extractor rejected a valid enum
fixture while the Lanius-built executable and separately GPU-built compiler
accepted it. Factoring that match into the shared predicate passes the connected
tests. This is not a diagnosis or fix of the underlying GPU compiler discrepancy.
The failing binary and source are retained under `target/verified-compiler/` as
`extractor-unique-inline-matches` and `ValueCollectionInlineMatches.lani`.

The final connected gate passes twenty Lean/x86 cases and seventeen independent
source-agreement audits, plus the five trap cases and fourteen malformed-source
cases. The Lanius-built extractor separately passes the nineteen-case gate
before the existing namespace fixture was added to it. The validated artifacts
are `extractor-direct`, `compiler-direct`, and `extractor-native.elf`; previous
versions are retained with the `before-unique` suffix.

Logs are `unique-{before,after}-perf.log`, `unique-connected.log`,
`unique-native-connected.log`, `unique-final-connected.log`,
`unique-visibility.log`, and `unique-self.log`.
The few-second Lean-admission target, general tool-correctness proofs, and
explicit execution/import contract remain unfinished.

## 2026-09-28: Diagnose and fix the reproduced GPU bootstrap discrepancy

The inline-match extractor failure above was not caused by duplicate-name
validation. In its generated x86, `add_value_candidate` constructed a five-word
nested enum but returned only three words. The lost payload made body lowering
reject a valid enum program. Diagnostic imports changed declaration positions
and hid the failure.

The GPU compiler's legacy nominal codes use `4096 + token` for structs and
`6144 + token` for enums. Declarations 2048 tokens apart can therefore share a
number. Layout projection preferred a numerically matching struct over the
enum that name resolution had actually selected.

The semantic artifact now retains resolved nominal identity for type syntax,
parameters, and annotated locals, following alias declaration edges as needed.
Variable uses reuse the existing declaration index rather than scanning HIR.
LIR layout consumes that identity and resolved call ABI before numeric fallback.
The fix changes two GPU shader sources; it adds no Rust compiler code, Lanius
workaround, output metadata, or Lean proof machinery. Legacy numeric fallbacks
remain elsewhere; this is not a claim that every possible collision is fixed.

`tests/gpu_nominal_identity_check.sh` checks the exact collision, neighboring
positions, and a two-level alias chain in GPU-built and Lanius-built x86. It
also checks that the colliding struct keeps its own layout. Generated padding
keeps the fixture short; two padding functions stay below the native parser's
1024-depth limit. Restoring the old shaders makes this test fail at the exact
collision. Seven existing aggregate/enum execution fixtures also pass.

The original failing extractor source now builds and emits the same `Some(7)`
Lean as the validated extractor. Rebuilt production tools pass all twenty
Lean/x86 cases, seventeen source-agreement audits, five trap cases, and fourteen
malformed-source cases. Full self-extraction is byte-identical to `SelfCore.lean`
(992 functions, 674 constants). The rebuilt Lanius compiler produces an
extractor ELF identical to the previously validated Lanius-built executable.

Validated GPU-built defaults are refreshed from `extractor-nominal-identity-final`
and `compiler-nominal-identity-final`; predecessors have the
`before-nominal-identity` suffix. Logs are `nominal-identity-{regression,old-shaders,
existing,final-connected,standard-build}.log` under `target/verified-compiler/`.
This diagnoses the inline-match failure, not the earlier parser-layout concern.
No new timing is claimed: the few-second admission target, general tool proofs,
and explicit execution/import contract remain open.

## 2026-09-28: Run shared extraction notation natively

The extraction notation was still running through Lean's interpreter. A native
library alone did not fix this: direct diagnostic builds had overwritten the
`Syntax` and `Link` `.olean` files without Lake's package metadata. Their native
symbol names therefore did not match. Rebuilding those two shared modules
through Lake restored matching metadata. Future isolated experiments must not
overwrite Lake-managed artifacts with ad hoc `lean -o` commands.

`formal/lakefile.lean` now provides `lake run checkExtraction -- <Lean args>`.
It builds the nine-module shared notation dependency graph, loads its native
libraries in dependency order, and forwards the arguments and exit status to
ordinary Lean. Library paths come from Lake, not hard-coded module lists or
platform-specific filenames. The connected emission test and full self-check
use this runner. Plain `lake env lean` remains valid; native loading is only
an execution optimization for the shared notation.

With shared infrastructure built first, identical fresh input used 109.96
billion checking instructions without native notation and 103.97 billion with
it, a 5.4% reduction. Paired wall times were 14.82 and 14.41 seconds. The
profiler's interpretation category fell to 2.87 ms, but most work remains in
ordinary Lean processing. No compilation, kernel checking, or artifact writing
was omitted or deferred, and no program-specific results were cached.

A reverse-order repeat without profiling confirmed the instruction reduction
(109.68 versus 103.75 billion), but wall time was worse with native notation
(21.15 versus 23.58 seconds). A final single-thread comparison on the same
pinned CPU used 28.40 versus 23.37 seconds of CPU time and 104.12 versus 89.92
billion cycles; wall times were 30.98 versus 23.66 seconds. This supports less
work, but those controlled single-thread times are not the normal parallel
checking latency. The repeat and pinned logs are also retained under
`native-notation-{repeat-baseline,runner,pinned-baseline,pinned-native}-perf.log`.

Complete evaluated Core snapshots match byte-for-byte (7,224,050 bytes): target,
entrypoint, nominal declarations, constants, and all functions. The connected
gate passes twenty Lean/x86 cases, seventeen source-agreement audits, five trap
cases, and fourteen malformed-source cases. The runner also rejects a false
theorem and preserves Lean's failing exit status.

The fresh current-source self-run remains 992 functions and 674 constants. Its
source is unchanged, its program definition has no axiom dependencies, and the
optional typing audit passes. It took 6.88 seconds to emit, 16.82 seconds to
check/write, and 29.40 seconds for the whole diagnostic. These wall times do
not establish a large speedup; lower instructions and controlled CPU time are
the evidence for retaining the native path. Logs are `native-notation-{baseline,native}-{perf,
check}.log`, `native-notation-connected.log`, and `native-notation-self.log`.

The generated Lean and Lanius implementation are unchanged. This work closes no
new general correctness theorem. The few-second target and the general tool
proofs and execution/import contract remain unfinished.

## 2026-09-28: Give named constants ownership of their values

The normal output previously emitted each constant twice in different forms:
a Core record in a private value table and a public name/ID-only `ConstantRef`.
It now emits one public declaration, for example:

```lean
def Extracted.«app::main».Constants.«answer» : Lanius.TypedIR.Surface.Constant
    Lanius.TypedIR.Surface.I32 :=
  constant (id := 0) 7
```

Its `.core` field owns the ID, type, and value; `.id` supports uses in function
bodies, and `.pack` supplies the same declaration to the program. Proofs can
refer directly to `answer.core.value`. The private list now contains references
to these declarations, not another serialized copy of their values. The old
Surface `ConstantRef` API was removed and its internal consumers migrated;
no compatibility alias or typing-proof layer was added.

The literal notation constructs ordinary Core data directly. It supports signed
limits, booleans, UTF-8 strings, unsigned values, and pointers. The Lanius printer
also emits a 64-bit unsigned value as one hexadecimal numeral, not a sum of two
storage words. Boundary tests cover negative storage limbs, the high bit, the
maximum unsigned value, and insufficient output capacity. This does not extend
the source frontend: its current constant-expression evaluator still rejects
decimal magnitudes above the signed-i32 initializer range, including large
`usize` initializers. High-bit formatting is tested directly at the printer and
Lean-literal boundaries, not claimed as new source-language support.

On the same frozen 80-unit input, complete evaluated Core snapshots match
byte-for-byte. Fresh checking with native shared notation used 104.02 versus
101.78 billion instructions (2.2% fewer); wall times were 21.32 and 21.56 seconds.
The artifact decreased from 40,294,864 to 40,143,280 bytes. These results support
the simpler representation, not a substantial latency improvement.

The connected gate passes twenty Lean/x86 cases, seventeen source-agreement
audits, five trap cases, and fourteen malformed-source cases. The constant gate
also checks module ownership, unused constants, signed arithmetic and wrapping,
three payload kinds, and rejection of fourteen individually changed values.
Captured constants and literal-to-Core equations pass the focused Lean tests.
The Lanius-built extractor emits the full current self-module byte-identically
to the GPU-built extractor. Validated defaults are `extractor-direct` and
`extractor-native.elf`; predecessors have the `before-named-constants` suffix.
The x86 compiler's source closure did not change.

The fresh current-source run contains 990 functions and 674 constants, reflecting
two deleted printer helpers. It took 6.25 seconds to emit, 18.07 seconds to
check/write, and 31.41 seconds for the complete diagnostic; the optional typing
audit passes and the program definition has no axiom dependencies. Logs are
`named-constants-{before,after}-{check,perf}.log`, `named-constants-public.log`,
`named-constants-connected.log`, and `named-constants-self.log`.

No new general correctness theorem is claimed. The few-second target, general
tool proofs, and explicit execution/import contract remain unfinished.

## 2026-09-28: Declare each function once

The emitter no longer writes a separate `FunctionRef`/`.ref` declaration for
every function. Calls use the function's own public name. The shared
`mutual functions … end` command reads IDs from those existing headers and
delegates signature and declaration elaboration to Lean's ordinary `mutual`.
Its temporary name-to-ID index is scoped to the block and is not serialized;
it holds neither bodies nor typing facts. Call nodes store ordinary Core IDs,
so source recursion does not become recursive Lean definitions. Captured and
previously defined function values use their own `core.id`.

The Surface `FunctionRef` API and both Lanius reference-emission helpers were
deleted, with internal consumers migrated. No compatibility alias, second
program tree, per-call signature check, axiom, or compilation bypass was added.
Ordinary type/constant lookup excludes the forward-function scope, while
callee lookup retains Lean's usual namespace resolution. Captured Lean locals
remain available. The command's `functions` token is not a reserved identifier.

The first prototype increased instructions from 105.82 to 117.36 billion.
Excluding irrelevant forward declarations from label/constant lookup and
avoiding repeated scope erasure reduced that to 106.89 billion: about 1% above
the baseline, not an instruction-count speedup. Wall times varied from 16.24
to 26.16 seconds, so do not claim a latency improvement from this change.
Logs are `function-declarations-{before,after,scope,empty-scope}-{check,perf}.log`.

On the same frozen 80-unit input, the actual printer's complete evaluated Core
matches the preceding format byte-for-byte (7,224,050 bytes). Source decreases
from 2,326,511 to 2,103,254 bytes; the `.olean` decreases from 40,143,280 to
39,062,664 bytes. The actual emitted module checked/wrote in 18.57 seconds.
The comparison artifacts are `FunctionDeclarationsActual.{lean,olean,core}`.

The connected gate passes twenty Lean/x86 cases, seventeen source-agreement
audits, five trap cases, and fourteen malformed-source cases. Focused checks
cover forward/nested/recursive calls, private names, module collisions,
captured functions and variables, and completed declarations used outside the
block. An arbitrary constructor with misleading `id` arguments is rejected.
The current self-run contains 988 functions and 674 constants, reflecting the
two deleted emitter helpers. It takes 5.90 seconds to emit, 16.47 seconds to
check/write, and 28.09 seconds for the diagnostic including setup and the
optional 439 ms typing audit. The program definition has no axiom dependencies.
Logs are `single-functions-{actual-check,connected,public,self}.log`.

The Lanius-built extractor self-extracts byte-identically to the GPU-built one;
the result is `SingleFunctionsNativeSelf.lean`. The runtime import gate also
passes, including changed bindings/signatures and omitted/escaped ABI names
(`single-functions-runtime.log`). Validated defaults are refreshed as
`extractor-direct` and `extractor-native.elf`; preceding executables have the
`before-single-functions` suffix. The x86 compiler source closure is unchanged.

This is a representation cleanup, not a new general correctness theorem or
the few-second admission target. General tool proofs and the explicit
execution/import contract remain unfinished.

An environment/IR census found 5,325 declarations under the 269 native source

The Lanius nominal-type emitter now surrounds just those declarations with
`section`, `set_option genSizeOf false`, and `end`. It still emits real Lean
inductives with their constructors, recursors, and equality lemmas. Subsequent
user declarations retain normal generation options. A proof that needs a size
can use Lean's standard `deriving instance SizeOf for SomeType`; no custom
compatibility layer or replacement proof library was added. The public enum
test checks injectivity, constructor distinctness, restored defaults, and this
explicit opt-in on the actual extracted type.

On identical 80-unit input, fresh checking uses 108.16 versus 100.51 billion
instructions (7.1% fewer), with paired wall times of 18.64 and 16.07 seconds.
The artifact decreases from 38,947,632 to 37,737,928 bytes. Shared notation was
already built; both runs checked, compiled, and wrote the complete fresh module.
No compiler pass or kernel check was disabled. Complete evaluated Core snapshots
match byte-for-byte (7,202,834 bytes), and all compiled-IR declaration/node counts
are unchanged. This is not a claim of byte-identical machine code: an optional
IR pretty-print diagnostic exceeded its 15-second bound and was discarded.

The option-only prototype logs are `size-of-{before,after}-{check,perf}.log`.
The actual printer's scoped version is checked in `lean-types-printed-{check,perf}.log`;

diagnostic including setup and the optional 401 ms typing audit. The program
definition has no axiom dependencies. These current-source wall times do not
establish an emission speedup; the fixed-input instruction reduction is the
evidence for retaining the change.

Twenty Lean/x86 cases, seventeen source-agreement audits, five trap cases, and
fourteen malformed-source cases pass, along with the native proof-support tests.
Logs are `lean-types-{example-check,proof-support,connected,self}.log`.
The Lanius-built extractor self-extracts byte-identically to the GPU-built one
(`LeanTypesNativeSelf.lean`). Validated defaults are `extractor-direct` and

x86 compiler source closure is unchanged.
No general compiler/typechecker/extractor correctness theorem is completed here.

## 2026-09-28: Reject callee-scope and scheduling micro-optimizations

The next profile used the frozen 988-function module from `size-of-after/`,
with native shared notation already built. A fresh baseline took 17.16 seconds
and 103.83 billion instructions. Sampling showed costs spread across allocation,
name comparison, compilation, and serialization; callee resolution was not the
dominant cost. Frame-pointer stacks were incomplete, so their inclusive sample
percentages must not be treated as a precise phase breakdown.

A prototype retained Lean's native resolver but narrowed its local context to
names mentioned in each body. Generic Lean elaboration retained the full scope,
and dependent contexts used the unchanged path. The shared notation tests and
full module checked, but instructions fell only to 102.35 billion (1.4%), while
wall time rose to 17.90 seconds. The extra name/scope machinery was removed;
`Syntax.lean` was compared byte-for-byte with its pre-experiment copy and its
native shared library rebuilt. No production code change survives this trial.

With the restored implementation, one Lean worker took 18.16 seconds and
102.17 billion instructions; synchronous elaboration took 17.62 seconds and
99.90 billion instructions. Neither establishes a useful latency improvement,
so the normal runner's scheduling remains unchanged. These trials did not
omit checking, compilation, or artifact writing. Logs are
`callee-{before,after}-{fresh,perf}.log`, `callee-before.data`,
`worker-one-{check,perf}.log`, and `elab-sync-{check,perf}.log`.

This rules out further investment in body-specific scope pruning and these
scheduling settings for the present workload. Ordinary Lean compilation and
artifact construction remain larger targets. The earlier constructor-only
compiler-pass experiment also failed to justify its complexity; do not repeat
it without a new cost model. No speedup or general correctness theorem is
claimed from this checkpoint.

Tracing the validation path found that the connected twenty-program gate ran

`direct_emission_check.sh` now emits each program through the default path and
uses that program for the existing execution, public-API, and source-agreement
checks. Checked output lives in a separate test-only namespace: it supplies
frontend evidence, and two `rfl` checks require identical Core programs and
entrypoints across modes. A guard also rejects frontend evidence in normal
output. No extra representation or checking step was added to production.

Both validated extractors pass twenty default Lean/x86 cases, twenty mode
agreement checks, seventeen exact source audits, five traps, and fourteen
malformed-source cases. The same-typed field/initializer mutations are now
checked against default output too. The GPU-built extractor's gate took 24.28
seconds and the Lanius-built extractor's gate took 25.66 seconds. These are
whole-suite timings, not fresh self-module admission results.

A diagnostic wrapper changed only normal `Some(7)` output to `Some(8)`, leaving
`--lean-checked` output untouched. The revised gate rejects it both at mode
equality and at execution; the old positive path did not consume that output.
Logs are `default-path-{connected,native,mutant}.log`. No production source
