The few-second target and general correctness/trusted-import work remain open.

## 2026-09-28: Primitive labels cannot be shadowed by source types

A naming review reproduced an extraction bug with a source record named `Bool`
whose field has primitive type `bool`. The emitter wrote an unqualified `Bool`
inside the generated inductive declaration, which Lean resolved to the source
record itself. `String` behaved the same way. The old module elaborated, and
the x86 program returned 7, but the generated constructor had the wrong argument
type. Result-only execution tests cannot establish preservation of these types.

`lean_syntax.named_type_ref` now emits `_root_.Bool`, `_root_.String`, and
`_root_.Unit` for those primitive labels. Public source declaration names do not
change; no aliases, typing-proof trees, or new trust assumptions were added.
The new `surface_builtin_label_shadow` fixture checks both native constructor
types and joins the connected default-output/source-agreement gate. Its two
constructor-type checks fail on the preceding extractor's output and pass with
the fix. Logs are `label-shadow-{repro,before-check,after-check}.log`.

Both GPU-built and Lanius-built extractors pass twenty-one Lean/x86 cases,
twenty-one mode-agreement cases, eighteen exact source audits, five traps, and
fourteen malformed-source cases. These gates took 26.64 and 27.13 seconds;
logs are `root-labels-{connected,native-connected}.log`. On the frozen 80-unit
input, the complete evaluated Core is byte-identical to the prior snapshot
(7,202,834 bytes), recorded in `root-label-frozen.core`. The current native
extractor also emits byte-identical self-output to the GPU-built one
(`RootLabelsNativeSelf.lean`). Validated default binaries are refreshed, with
predecessors named `before-root-labels`.

The self-check script now invalidates only `CurrentSourceClosure`'s build trace
and rebuilds it through Lake with cache downloads disabled. This refreshes its
untracked `include_str` inputs without overwriting a Lake-managed `.olean`
outside the package build and losing native symbol metadata. The logged rebuild
succeeded in 1.0 second. The full current self-run remains 988 functions and
674 constants: 5.80 seconds for emission, 15.87 for fresh Lean check/compile/write,
and 27.36 for the diagnostic including setup and the optional 430 ms typing

record. A focused equality regression failed before the fix. The current
Lanius printer already emits declaration order, so this was a notation bug,
not evidence that its existing generated records had the wrong values.

evidence. An explicit mismatched-type test continues to produce ordinary Core
data. Existing field projection and record construction share the same small
constructor-field reader; no second field table or format was added.

The production change adds 12 net Lean lines. The 67 added test lines cover
permuted and nested fields, aliases, applied labels, malformed names, arbitrary
callee order, and reconstruction using the actual imported generated types.
The focused record check takes 0.82 seconds with shared infrastructure built.
The connected gate passes 25 Lean/x86 cases, 22 exact source audits, 11 traps,
and 16 malformed-source cases in 37.14 seconds. Complete self Core remains
byte-identical across all 6,978,158 snapshot bytes. Fresh admission uses 74.57
billion instructions versus the preceding 74.66; no material change in work
is established. Its 12.60-second wall time is a single fixed-input check, not

`target/verified-compiler/record-names/`.

The preceding native profile mainly identified allocation and reference-count

## 2026-09-28: Attribute string allocations; repair packed string semantics

An instruction-address syscall trace of the current native extractor separates
its 421,349 mappings into 4,556 ordinary allocations and 416,793 string-copy
allocations. Disassembly identifies the latter as the allocator embedded in
`runtime::string::pointer`, not the public allocation service. Evidence lives
in `target/verified-compiler/allocation-attribution/native-sites.txt`. This
confirms the excess allocation source; it does not justify replacing fresh
copies with aliased literal pointers. No allocator or startup ABI was changed.

Inspecting that contract exposed a correctness gap more important than the
syscall cost. The Lanius compiler reads string bytes as packed i32 words. Its
native runtime copies into fresh zero-filled storage with room for the last
word, but `mapStringDataPtr` recorded only the literal's exact byte count. A
minimal extracted program reading one word from `"%"` returned 37 natively and
trapped with `rawMemoryBounds` in Lean. The failure was reproduced before the
model change.

The model now names the existing packed-byte storage contract explicitly:
`stringStorage` appends zero bytes to the next four-byte boundary. Logical
string values and lengths do not change. General kernel-checked lemmas prove
that taking the original byte length recovers the UTF-8 contents and that the
storage length is `((byteLength + 3) / 4) * 4`. Fresh allocation, mutation
isolation, borrowed ownership, and ordinary exact-block slice bounds remain
unchanged. This is a representation correction, not removal of bounds checks.

Focused model tests cover byte-length residues, empty strings, embedded NUL,
UTF-8, and rejection beyond the padded extent. The connected fixtures cover
multiple word boundaries, fresh pointers (including empty strings), mutation
isolation, and the actual extractor's `lean_output::text`. The latter checks a
non-word-aligned destination, UTF-8, neighboring-byte preservation, and an
insufficient-capacity failure without modifying output.

Shared model/execution rebuilding took 3.03 seconds; the affected checker
rebuild took 11.27 seconds. The expanded string execution proof checks in
1.87 seconds and the actual writer proof in 1.44 seconds, with no
`native_decide` or new axioms. Production semantics gains 16 lines, including
the two general lemmas; focused new test files total 88 lines. The connected
gate passes 27 Lean/x86 cases, 22 source audits, 11 traps, and 16 malformed
inputs in 52.79 seconds. Raw-string/raw-slice fixtures remain outside the
independent source checker's supported fragment; their Core execution is
checked directly against native results.

There is no new emission/admission speedup to report. The previous fresh
self-module timings remain the latest performance checkpoint. General tool
correctness, the native storage refinement, the explicit trusted-output
mechanism, and the few-second total target remain open. Any future string
arena must preserve fresh identities and zero padding and include its runtime
state/layout obligations; do not silently enlarge the startup ABI to hide
this cost.

## 2026-09-28: Connect byte arithmetic and rejection to fresh writer output

The new proof increment follows `verified::lean_output::byte`, not the old
integer-certificate emitter. Its packed-word update must preserve all three
neighboring bytes even when the destination is nonzero or negative. The shared
`OutputPacking.Byte` proof establishes this for every 32-bit word, every byte,
and all four lanes. It also connects bit lanes to `i32Bytes`, the serializer
used by native heap stores. No zero-tail assumption or native decision
procedure is used.

`Semantics.Bitwise` connects Core's signed i32 AND, OR, XOR, and valid left shifts
to bitvector operations. The existing signed-wrap lemma moved there from the
x86 expression checker; its internal consumer imports the shared theorem,
without an alias or compatibility wrapper. Small fuel-stable expression and
return rules handle composition without unfolding the whole evaluator.

`tests/direct_output_byte.lean` is checked against fresh default output from
the actual writer and its dependencies. It proves two contracts:

- At the update expression, readable output and the computed mask/shift/local
  bindings imply the exact serialized byte replacement, with no expression
  side effects. Operand reads are proved from the bindings and heap read;
  successful execution of the update is not an assumption.
- At the whole extracted function body, a negative cursor, cursor at or beyond
  capacity, or byte outside 0..255 returns -1 with the entire state unchanged.
  This needs no readable or writable output-buffer assumption.

Fresh admission of the 18-function writer fixture took 0.64 seconds; its
connected contracts then checked in 0.54 seconds with shared infrastructure
built. Their only axiom dependencies are Lean's standard `propext`,
`Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`. A changed OR-to-AND update still elaborated
as ordinary Core data but failed the connected proof. The affected x86
expression tests and shared scalar/composition modules also pass.

The addition is 91 lines of source-specific connected proof/check code and
231 net lines of shared production proof support, counting physical lines,
comments, and the moved lemma's deletion. That support includes bit arithmetic,
the serializer connection, and evaluator rules; it is not free just because it
is shared. Do not multiply this into separate instruction traces for each
writer. The remaining success proof should reuse a general memory-store
contract and establish the local arithmetic from the entry arguments.

The connected integration gate passes all 27 Lean/x86 cases, 22 source audits,
11 traps, and 16 malformed-source cases in 48.66 seconds. It now checks these
contracts whenever it freshly extracts the writer fixture, and explicitly
builds their shared dependencies. Existing pure-expression and return/sequence
proofs reuse the new rules rather than duplicating evaluator bookkeeping.

This does **not** finish `byte`: success-path bounds, initialization of the
locals, the actual memory write and its frame, return value, and call boundary
still need composition. The readable-heap premise at the update point must be
derived from the entry buffer contract. Nor does a proof about fresh extracted
Core establish that the extractor preserves arbitrary Lanius source; that
general implementation/provenance connection remains open. Compiler/emitter
instructions are unchanged, and no generation/admission speedup is claimed.
Evidence is under `target/verified-compiler/packing-proof/`.

## 2026-09-28: Connect the actual byte store; share memory and expression rules

`actualStore_correct` now proves the assignment selected from fresh default
extraction of `lean_output::byte`, including its destination. Given the current
operand bindings, an in-bounds raw-slice index, the four input bytes, unique
heap block bases, and no registered cell-backed i32 views, it derives successful
assignment and a one-byte heap effect. It does not assume successful execution
of the assignment or its store. A serializer/read inverse removes the previous
separate successful-read premise: the same input bytes justify both operations.

The shared memory contract preserves reads at every other address, including
raw aliases and invalid-address errors, and preserves allocation identity,
size, alignment, liveness, ownership, frontier, and remaining budget. The Core
assignment leaves locals, cells, cell frontier, external-world state, and views
unchanged in the no-registered-view case. This is not a claim of full heap
well-formedness preservation or a proof of registered-view synchronization.

A concrete instance uses a negative, nonzero word at a nonzero word index. It
checks the selected byte through two aliases, neighboring bytes, a canary in
another allocation, and an invalid-pointer read. Fresh writer admission took
0.68 seconds; the connected contracts checked in 0.71 seconds before the

## 2026-09-28: Keep raw i32 slices as native descriptors

Reviewing the writer proof's size exposed a representation problem before
more proof compression was justified. The normal host raw-parts intrinsic
produced a copied cell-backed array in Core, whereas slices loaded from native
records were already `Value.rawSlice` descriptors. The Lanius x86 branch
`backend::compile::raw_slice` only emits a pointer/length descriptor, and its
data-pointer branch reads that descriptor's pointer. This mismatch made the
writer's no-registered-view domain awkward to reach from normal raw allocation.

There was also an observable bug: `i32SliceDataPtr` rejected a raw descriptor
loaded from a record. The new `surface_raw_slice_alias.lani` program returned
37 on x86 but failed its Lean execution assertion before the change. Afterward
both return 37, and the connected Lean check takes 0.67 seconds. The fixture
checks pointer identity and mutations through the original, repeated, and
record-held aliases, not just the shape of a descriptor.

`mapRawI32Slice` now protects the allocation and returns a raw pointer/length
value. It neither reads/decodes the contents nor allocates a backing cell or
registered view. Negative lengths and exact allocation size, alignment,
liveness, and ownership checks remain. Actual accesses still check memory
bounds. The old raw-address view lookup and its reuse theorem were removed;
they had no other source consumers. Raw construction is no longer a payload
scan: callers relying on allocation validity must still establish heap
well-formedness rather than supply arbitrary inconsistent model blocks.

This does not remove the registry for genuine language arrays. Taking an
array's address still creates a cell-backed view, and existing synchronization
updates raw and array aliases in both directions. A focused executable `#eval`
regression checks this path; it is not a general aliasing proof and supplies no
axiom to any theorem. The constructor/error/empty/repeated-view checks remain
kernel-checked examples. Their focused build takes 0.86 seconds; rebuilding the
affected semantics, writer foundations, and existing regressions took 10.20
seconds separately.

`mapRawI32Slice_of_block` proves the exact constructor result from allocation
metadata, without assuming successful construction. `StableExpr.rawSliceDataPtr`
composes a descriptor's proved evaluation with pointer projection. Both use
only standard Lean logical axioms. The byte-writer regression now starts with
this constructor theorem and checks the public call for every byte and all
four lanes, preserving existing caller locals. Its connected contracts take
3.10 seconds with shared infrastructure built. The no-registered-view premise
still matters if the surrounding program exposes genuine language arrays; a
global no-view invariant for the entire extractor is not proved here.

The fresh-output integration gate passes 28 Lean/x86 cases, 22 source audits,
11 traps, and 16 malformed-source cases in 62.00 seconds. Raw-slice fixtures
remain outside the independent source checker's fragment. This is an executable
semantics repair and a better proof boundary, not general extraction/native
preservation or a new self-emission/admission speedup.

The representation change removes 33 net lines from the semantics and adds
28 lines of shared proof support: five fewer production lines overall. New
focused tests add 74 Lean and 27 Lanius lines. The writer file adds one import
and ten regression lines; its source-specific proof/glue is still 214 lines
for the eleven-line function, about 19 times. The compactness target remains
unmet. Do not rebuild a second backing store merely to reuse cell-based proofs;
raw buffer contracts should describe the authoritative heap. Evidence is under
`target/verified-compiler/raw-slice-model/`.

cursor. The domain requires readable packed buffers, adequate capacity below
the signed-i32 limit, disjoint input/output ranges, well-formed cells, and unique
heap block bases. A concrete multiword instance proves this domain inhabited.

Shared rules now compose terminating counted loops, reuse a value-argument call

extend exact byte-write prefixes. Signed shifts followed by the byte mask are

## 2026-09-29: Share loop/record rules and close two output contracts

The next unit of work remains correctness of the actual extractor, not another
emission-speed experiment. This checkpoint closes two needed leaf contracts and
removes repeated state mechanics before extending the printer proof.

`CountedLocal` is a generic invariant over arbitrary programs, variable IDs,
states, and action expressions. Its step rule composes a proved read and action
with the actual temporary binding and counter increment. It allows heap/world
effects while retaining preexisting cells, and handles scope restoration once.
The text iteration now uses it instead of duplicating counter lookup, cell
assignment, freshness, and scope proofs. Its full public success/rejection
contracts keep their previous domains and postconditions. A test shadows a
caller variable with the counter, then shadows that counter with a callee
parameter; the counter advances and restoring the caller recovers its old value.

`AggregateRun` supplies general record-construction and field-projection rules
without requiring purity or a typing tree. `core_run` composes fields in order
from their proved contracts. One test mutates a variable in the first field and
reads the new value in the second; reversing the order returns the old value
while retaining the mutation. A wrong projected result fails. These tests caught
a matching issue: the automation compared local contract types without first
normalizing their metadata and resolved metavariables. It now does so before
matching arguments. The kernel still checks the resulting ordinary proof;
the automation neither reexecutes the program nor trusts a native decision.

`OutputCursorProof.actualCall` proves that the actual constructor returns its
three input fields in order, using the actual nominal ID, and preserves the
caller through a `PureFrame`. It requires well-formed cells but no valid output
buffer: constructing a cursor does not access storage. Its body proof uses the
shared statement/record automation. `OutputHexProof.actualCall` proves that the
actual `hex_digit` returns the lowercase ASCII character for every `Fin 16`
input, preserving the caller. It does not claim correctness on arbitrary
non-nibble inputs; callers still need to establish the masking precondition.

## 2026-09-29: Close hex-word output, including partial-capacity failure

`OutputHexWordProof.actualCall_formats` proves the actual extracted `hex_word`
call, not a separately defined implementation. For a canonical `BitVec 32`
word and an output buffer with natural cursor position at most capacity,
capacity below 2^31, and readable packed storage covering that capacity, let
`n = min 8 (capacity - position)`. The call terminates, writes exactly the first
`n` lowercase hex digits at the cursor, and returns `position + 8` if `n = 8`,
otherwise -1. The byte frame preserves every other read, including aliases
and invalid addresses, allocation layout, frontier, and budget. Fresh-cell
framing preserves caller variables; world and registered views are unchanged.
The environmental assumptions are well-formed cells, unique heap block bases,
and no registered i32 views. Internal nibble calculations, digit calls, byte
calls, guard outcomes, loop termination, and final return are derived, not
assumed. Negative/past-capacity initial cursors remain outside this theorem;
the native regression covers them without claiming a general proof.

The source now uses one counter and derives the cursor and shift from it.
The old two-counter implementation and the revised implementation both pass
the same native capacities-0-through-8 regression, exact prefix/canary checks,
and invalid-cursor cases. An upfront all-space check would change the partial
write behavior and was not introduced. A bitwise lemma proves shift-and-mask
nibble selection for arbitrary words, including negative signed values.

`OutputState` shares the exact prefix, fresh counter/cells, readable output,
world, and view facts. Its loop rule composes continuing iterations with a
proved terminal loop (ordinary completion or capacity error). It does not
assume a successful target loop. Text copying now uses this same invariant
and derives input readability and unchanged bytes from `BytesWrite`, removing
its separate six-field copying-state record. General eager-binary composition
retains operand effects and order; tests distinguish reading before and after
a mutation and ensure short-circuit operators do not use that eager rule.
Parameter/local selectors derive IDs from actual function structure; nested
binding and shifted-ID tests pass. No per-program typing tree or proof data
was added to extraction.

writer module take 1.18 seconds for hex-word and 4.17 for text, including their

12.34, and combined writer contracts 10.14. Its actual embedding contains 950
functions and 667 constants. The 79.50-second connected gate passes 29 Lean/x86

and the extra native capacity-boundary fixture. All commands remain below two

## 2026-09-29: Share contract facts and remove caller encoding obligations

The actual byte success theorem now accepts `ByteBuffer state` with four
readable bytes at the selected packed-word address, plus a `Fin 256` byte.
It reconstructs the old word internally using the existing `readableWord`
lemma. This replaces the previous caller-supplied word/load equality with
the equivalent readable-storage domain; it does not assume successful
execution or strengthen clients to require unrelated readable storage.
The theorem still proves the actual store, exact byte effect, return, fresh
cells, world, and views. Existing alias, neighboring-byte, invalid-address,
raw-constructor, all-four-lane, and arbitrary-byte instances still pass.
Byte rejection is unchanged.

`core_facts [context, frame]` introduces proved proposition fields by record
projection, including nested records to depth three. It neither runs the
program nor reconstructs typing. The registered `CountedLocal.read` rule
lets `core_local` transport lookups without handwritten aliases. Fresh-cell
formation composes through `core_formed`; scalar guards compose from proved
call results through `core_run`. The hex writer no longer restates local
lookups, fresh-cell formation, and both byte-result guard outcomes. Text
uses the same fact/lookup machinery and derives both loop guards directly.
Its final bounded addition still uses the explicit arithmetic theorem;
the attempted broader scalar normalization was not retained.

The full hex and text contracts keep their prior domains and conclusions,
including partial hex output on capacity failure, exact UTF-8 copying,
termination, and memory/caller framing. `ProofFactsTests` rejects invented
bounds, wrong counter values, and shadowed lookups. `AggregateRunTests`
checks effectful comparisons and rejects the opposite guard result; prior
field-order and short-circuit failures still pass. The focused expression,
counter, call, binding-selector, and record tests pass. No new axioms,
native proof computation, emitted proof data, or production Lanius changes
were added.

Fresh writer extraction and all six byte/packing/cursor/digit/hex/text proof
files pass. Under concurrent validation load, hex takes 3.37 seconds and text
6.23. The self gate passes in 36.26 seconds: emission 3.89, module admission
11.94, combined writer contracts 15.04, plus setup. The embedding still has
950 functions and 667 constants. These are validation timings, not controlled
performance comparisons; only `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`
appear in successful contract axiom reports. The previous connected x86
gate remains the native-execution checkpoint; it was not rerun for these
proof-only changes.

The internal mutation harness is now `tests/output_contract_mutations.sh`;
the old hex-only filename was removed, with no wrapper. It changes one
emitted function at a time: hex nibble selection, increment, rejection
return, or success return; text input string, increment, or final cursor.
Every changed module admits as ordinary Lean data, while the relevant
public contract fails with exit 1 and no olean. The harness also checks
that the expected public contract, rather than an unrelated proof, failed.
An initial all-writer bundle reached its 110-second guard after six cases;
the retained harness checks only the affected writer and its dependencies.
The narrowed seven-case check passes in 72.05 seconds under concurrent load.

## 2026-09-29: Require division safety in the actual x86 step relation

An audit ruled out a suspected Core/native arithmetic mismatch: Core already
traps on signed minimum divided or reduced modulo -1, and the native gate
already covers both cases. No arithmetic source change was retained.

The audit found a different correctness hole. `Machine.Step.decoded` admitted
every decoded instruction using the total `execute` function. That included
DIV/IDIV with a zero divisor or an out-of-range quotient, bypassing
`Step.divided` and its `DivisionSuccess` premise. The generic rule now requires
`instruction.isDivision = false`. All division successes must use the existing
checked rule; its exact quotient/remainder and range obligations are unchanged.
Ordinary encoding proofs supply the structural fact directly, with no
guardless compatibility wrapper. This changes the proof model, not Lanius
runtime code or normal emitted Lean.

The divisor/range requirements agree with Intel's
[DIV/IDIV specification](https://www.intel.com/content/dam/www/public/us/en/documents/manuals/64-ia-32-architectures-software-developer-vol-2a-manual.pdf):
zero divisors and quotient overflow cause a divide exception, not a successful
instruction transition. Other machine-model environmental assumptions remain,
including mapped/protected memory and the supported ISA fragment.

`DecodedDivisionTests` rejects generic execution for arbitrary states and
divisors, both widths, and both signedness modes. It retains positive checks
for successful checked division and for zero/overflow faults. The mutation
harness copies the actual relation into an isolated namespace: the guarded
copy passes, while replacing its guard with `True` makes the negative
regression fail with exit 1 and no olean. Production sources and their build
artifacts are not mutated. Both checks together take 1.43 seconds.

The focused machine, encoding, frame-slot, literal-return, and comparison gate
passes in 11.59 seconds, including affected shared dependency builds. Connected
program checking and ELF startup/exit proofs pass in 20.36 seconds. An additional
1.91-second cached-dependency check audits the two startup/exit theorems; they
and the division/fault theorems depend only on `propext`, `Classical.choice`,
and `Quot.sound`. These are proof-model validation times, not fresh program
extraction timings or speedup claims. No whole-extractor native gate was needed
because neither source compilation nor emitted data changed.

Matching-scope growth is eight production Lean lines, 39 new regression lines,
and two axiom-audit lines: 49 Lean lines total. The shell mutation harness is
counted separately. This is an integrity fix, not a compactness improvement.
The persistent goal remains active: whole-printer preservation, general
frontend/lowering correctness, complete x86/runtime refinement, verified
self-hosting, and the explicit trusted-output boundary remain unfinished.
The current `VERIFICATION.md` remaining-work list now describes this direct
Lean path instead of treating legacy certificate production as the target.

Evidence is under `target/verified-compiler/decoded-division/`: `after.log`,
`connected.log`, `axioms.log`, and `mutation/{guarded,unchecked}.log`.

## 2026-09-29: Reuse call setup and prove decimal input rejection

`core_call declaration` sets up the actual value-argument call using
`RunsExpr.callUnchanged`. It derives parameter IDs from the declaration,
constructs concrete bindings once, and proves lookup, binding equality, and
cell formation with existing kernel-checked rules. It does not prove the body
by assuming execution; the body is the remaining goal. Wrong arity, a missing
function, an invented result, and effectful arguments cannot pass this helper.
The first implementation retained a symbolic zip/map binding expression;
the final implementation materializes the pairs so local lookups do not
repeatedly reduce that expression. This is proof authoring for the tool itself,
not a new typing or proof tree in ordinary extracted output.

`core_arguments` composes expression-list contracts in evaluation order.
`core_run` uses it with `RunsExpr.callArguments` and a supplied value-call
contract, without reopening the function body. The effectful regression proves
that a second argument sees the first argument's mutation, retains the caller's
cell binding through parameter shadowing, and rejects an invented return.
Existing record-field order and short-circuit tests still pass.

Cursor, hex-digit, packed length, and byte rejection now use the call helper.
Hex and text use argument composition for their nested digit, packing, and
byte calls. Existing public domains and conclusions are unchanged, including
partial hex output, exact text bytes, termination, and full caller/memory frames.
Some arithmetic normalization remains explicit; this is not a general loop
or heap-proof synthesizer.

`OutputNaturalProof.actualCall_rejects` proves the actual decimal writer's
initial guard for all values/positions/capacities satisfying
`value < 0 ∨ position < 0 ∨ capacity < position`, from well-formed caller cells.
It returns -1 and a `PureFrame`; no valid output storage or successful internal
execution is assumed. The two-line proof uses the actual extracted declaration.
An empty-state example permits a non-slice output argument. This contract does
not cover digit counting, capacity failure after counting, or successful decimal
output. Do not count the sixth writer as complete.

Fresh standalone checks against a freshly emitted writer fixture pass for
byte, cursor, hex-digit, packed length, hex-word, text, and decimal rejection.
The final self gate passes in 43.76 seconds under concurrent mutation load:
emission 4.45, module admission 13.62, combined writer contracts 19.52, plus
source setup and the separate 400 ms typing audit. It still has 950 functions
and 667 constants. The ordinary program has no axioms; the successful writer
contracts and focused automation theorem use only `propext`, `Classical.choice`,
and `Quot.sound`. Shared call/argument/record tests pass in 2.72 seconds,
including affected dependency builds. These are validation timings, not a
controlled speedup. No native gate was repeated for these proof-only changes.

The mutation harness now checks nine wrong emitted functions: the previous
four hex and three text mutations, plus weakened decimal rejection and a wrong
decimal rejection return. All admit as ordinary data but fail the relevant
public contract with exit 1 and no olean. The final run takes 90.97 seconds.
Both self and connected fixture gates build/import the new shared helper;
the new rejection proof is included in their writer bundles.

## September 29: Decimal width and capacity rejection

The next obligation was the decimal writer's counting loop, not another
per-program validation pass. Nonnegative i32 values need one to ten digits.
The Lanius implementation now computes that width with a small decision tree
(at most four comparisons), removing the counting loop and its repeated
division. It retains the existing public arguments, capacity behavior, and
right-to-left digit-writing loop. The private helper adds one extracted
function; the ordinary emission format does not change.

`OutputNaturalProof.decimalWidth_spec` proves minimal width for all
`value < 2^31`, including zero. `actualWidth` proves that the actual extracted
helper returns that width and a pure caller frame, without assuming an
internal execution result. `actualCall_noSpace` composes the helper with the
actual writer: well-formed caller cells, canonical nonnegative capacity,
`position ≤ capacity`, and too little remaining space imply return -1 with
no output access. Output storage need not be valid. Fresh parameter and
temporary cells are allowed; old cells, caller locals, heap, world, and views
remain unchanged. Empty-storage and maximum-i32 examples witness the domain.
Initial invalid-input rejection remains proved. Successful formatting and
the remaining digit loop are not proved; keep writer completion at five of six.

Shared `RunsExpr.callPure` and `RunsStmt.letLocalPure` compose proved body and
initializer frames. `core_call_pure … as callee` derives parameter bindings
from the actual declaration and leaves the framed body as an explicit proof
obligation. Existing unchanged-body calls reuse the new rule. Tests cover
fresh local allocation, restored caller scope, and rejection of an invented
return. No evaluator, typing checker, native computation, or extra axiom is
used to discharge these contracts.

The width proof exposed proof-search failures on symbolic comparisons with
large numeric thresholds. Binary rules no longer try `decide` on arithmetic
evaluation equalities. Fixed-result comparisons normalize to arithmetic
instead of attempting reflexivity that unfolds a large numeral before
failing. Statement sequencing checks non-fallthrough before recursively
proving a completed prefix. Focused expression, statement, call, and aggregate
tests pass, including left-to-right mutation and wrong-result negatives.

Both changed tools bootstrap with the GPU compiler to x86 executables
(extractor 2.32 seconds; compiler 2.25). A native property fixture covers zero,
both sides of every power-of-ten threshold, maximum i32, every relevant short
capacity, exact output bytes, and untouched surrounding bytes. It passes
against both the old and new implementations. The connected gate includes it.

Fresh writer admission and all four decimal contracts pass; the decimal proof
file checks in 9.83 seconds. The fresh 951-function, 667-constant self gate
passes in 58.70 seconds: emission 4.84, admission 17.84, writer contracts 28.16,
plus setup and the separate 402 ms typing diagnostic. Shared infrastructure
was built by Lake; program output and its contracts were freshly checked.
The program has no axioms. Successful contracts use only `propext`,
`Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound` (the width specification uses no choice).

Three new mutations change a width threshold, the ten-digit return, or the
capacity guard. All admit as ordinary data but fail the relevant public

The first broad connected run hit its 110-second guard under concurrent load.
A traced run without that load passes in 102.31 seconds: 29 default-output

11 trap cases, and malformed-body/duplicate-callable rejections also pass.

## September 29: Complete decimal writer contract

`OutputNaturalProof.actualCall_formats` now proves successful formatting of
the actual `natural` function in the emitted Core. For any nonnegative i32
value and valid packed output with enough space, it terminates, writes exactly
the minimal-width decimal bytes, and returns `position + decimalWidth value`.
Its output model uses positional powers of ten, not a copy of the loop. The
width specification proves minimality, including one digit for zero. Existing
initial-input and insufficient-space rejection contracts remain checked.

The success domain requires capacity below 2^31, readable packed storage,
well-formed caller cells, unique heap block bases, and no registered views.
The proof derives every internal assignment, arithmetic bound, and byte store.
It does not assume successful intermediate execution. The exact byte frame
preserves reads through every alias and invalid pointer outside the output
range, as well as allocation metadata. Fresh parameter and temporary cells
are allowed; original caller cells, scope, world, and views are retained.
All six basic output routines now have their stated general call contracts.
This proves the extracted Core functions, not general source-to-Core agreement
or the entire Lean printer.

Two shared rules avoid a second evaluator proof for each scalar assignment:
`StableExpr.assignI32` composes a proved RHS and scalar operation with actual
place resolution; `RunsExpr.assignI32` derives the updated state's existence.
`LocalPair` tracks two distinct fresh mutable cells while preserving the old
cell prefix and transporting old local lookups. Its tests include repeated
updates and rejection of aliased local IDs. `BytesWrite.cons` composes a
right-hand output suffix followed by a left-hand byte, retaining the full
heap frame. The decimal loop uses these rules with a decreasing digit-count
invariant; the public call composes width calculation, capacity guard, local
scopes, the loop, return, and caller restoration. No normal-output format,
typing-proof tree, certificate, native proof discharge, or new axiom was added.

The final standalone decimal file passes in 12.60 seconds against the fresh
writer embedding, with shared infrastructure already built. Regressions cover
zero and maximum-i32 bytes, and instantiate the successful call contract for
every value in `Fin (2^31)` using an unaligned buffer, a shadowed caller local,
and surrounding/invalid-pointer frames. Focused shared-rule and existing
automation tests pass (the final cached Lake run takes 0.56 seconds).

The fresh self gate passes in 60.42 seconds without concurrent mutation load:
emission 4.41, module admission 15.70, combined writer contracts 32.88, plus
shared/source setup and the separate 321 ms typing diagnostic. It contains
951 functions and 667 constants. `extractedProgram` has no axioms; successful
contracts use only `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`. The self and
connected gates build/import the new shared modules, and self proof assembly
collects the decimal dependencies. Normal self output remains proof-free.

Four focused mutations alter the emitted decimal digit, decrement, quotient,
or successful return. Each mutated program admits as ordinary data, but its
public formatting contract fails with exit 1 and no olean (83.80 seconds for
all four). The harness includes the byte contract needed by decimal success.
Older unrelated mutations were not rerun. The native capacity fixture also

## September 29: Signed printer composition

`OutputSignedProof.actualCall_formats` proves the actual extracted
`verified::lean_syntax::signed` function for every canonical i32, including
-2147483648. Given readable packed output with enough capacity, it terminates,
writes its decimal spelling, and returns the next byte position. It derives
the internal calls from the already checked byte, natural, and text contracts;
successful intermediate execution is not a caller premise. The theorem uses
the actual self-extracted compiler module, not a substitute implementation.
The output model is a sign followed by positional decimal digits; it does not
copy the implementation's special-case minimum-value string branch.

The domain requires capacity below 2^31, well-formed caller cells, no registered
views, unique heap block bases, and existing blocks below the allocation
frontier. The last condition supports the minimum-value branch's fresh string
storage. The contract preserves caller cells, world, views, allocation budget,
and every old successful read outside the output. It does not claim that
previously invalid addresses remain invalid: fresh string storage can make
them readable. This is a successful-formatting contract; signed-writer
insufficient-space and invalid-descriptor behavior are not yet proved here.

`Memory.OutputEffect` composes exact byte writes and fresh borrowed storage.
Its rules derive exact final output, old-memory framing, readability, unique
block bases, allocation bounds, and unchanged budget. `WriterFrame` adds caller
cell/world/view framing. Its call and local-scope rules handle parameter cells
and temporary scope once. The extended call tactic uses a supplied checked
framing rule and leaves the body as a proof obligation. These are ordinary
semantic proofs, not a runtime plan, emitted certificate, second typing tree,
new axiom, or kernel-checking bypass. No production Lanius changed.

Focused shared tests pass, including text followed by a byte, fresh storage
with a canary, framed calls, and rejection of a wrong return value. The signed
regression instantiates the public theorem for every canonical i32 in an
unaligned buffer with a shadowed caller binding and neighboring allocation.
It checks exact loaded bytes and both canaries, including the borrowed-storage
branch. Fixed examples cover minimum i32, -1, zero, and maximum i32.

The standalone signed file passes in 4.45 seconds with the existing self-module,
basic-writer proof artifact, and shared infrastructure already built. Fresh
self-extraction and connected writer checking pass in 52.41 seconds in the
final run: emission 4.09, module admission 12.11, basic writer contracts 26.25,
signed contract 4.30, plus setup and the separate 317 ms typing diagnostic.
An earlier pass took 61.52 seconds. The self module
still contains 951 functions and 667 constants. `extractedProgram` has no
axioms; successful contracts use only standard Lean logical axioms. These are
validation timings, not a claimed speedup. The self gate now produces the
basic-writer proof artifact with its own module root, then checks signed
composition against it. The broad native connected suite was not repeated
for proof-only changes.

## September 29: Shared IR traversal contracts

The Lean renderer and x86 emitter use the same indexed `ir::body_tree::Tree`.
`IRNavigationProof` now proves the actual self-extracted `subtree_end` and
`next_sibling` calls, rather than a replacement traversal implementation.
Range rejection returns -1 without backing-memory access, including invalid
descriptors. In-range subtree lookup returns the stored endpoint. Sibling
lookup returns its candidate exactly when it advances past the current node
and remains strictly before the parent's endpoint; otherwise it returns -1.
The calls terminate and preserve the caller's existing cells, heap, world,
views, and lexical scope, while allowing fresh temporary local cells.

`EndTable` is a logical view of the existing packed i32 endpoint slice. It
relates each element to actual heap bytes, requires an i32-sized count, and
records nonnegative endpoints bounded by that count. Successful reads follow
from those bytes, not from an assumed successful evaluation of the function.
Unused event and annotation fields remain arbitrary because these two APIs
do not read them. The successful-sibling model yields a strict forward step
inside the parent and a decreasing count-minus-index measure.

The bounds and storage facts are still caller assumptions. This work does
not prove that `Tree.build` constructs them or that `Tree.valid` implies them.
It also does not yet prove event arities, first-child correctness, complete
child enumeration, recursive printing, or source/IR/Surface correspondence.
The next structural obligation is to derive these navigation assumptions
from the actual tree constructor/validator, then use them in the renderer's
whole-tree preservation proof. A second emitted tree is not needed.

Shared `core_pure` rules compose read-only initializers, local scope, conditionals,
fallthrough, and early return. They leave every expression, read, branch, and
frame as a kernel-checked obligation. Mutation and effectful initializers
require separate proved contracts. Closed scalar normalization handles only
scalar operations, not program execution. Field projection and indexed reads
reuse existing semantic rules. No production Lanius, normal emitted Lean,
integer certificate, typing-proof tree, native proof discharge, or trust
policy changed.

Focused tests cover nested shadowing, early return before a trap, fallthrough,
wrong return values, skipped traps, and a real successful mutation incorrectly
claimed as pure. Packed-tree examples cover an advancing sibling, the last
child's rejection, an existing shadowed caller cell, and zero-count rejection
with an invalid backing pointer. Shared scalar/field regressions retain
wrong-result rejection and existing effect-order tests.

Evidence under `target/verified-compiler/ir-navigation/`:

- `navigation.log`: all general contracts and packed examples pass in 4.53
  seconds against the existing self-module with shared dependencies built.
- `self-final.log`: fresh self-extraction and connected writer/navigation
  proofs pass in 65.41 seconds. Emission takes 4.04, admission 14.20, basic
  writers 30.88, signed printing 4.16, and navigation 4.31 seconds. The
  separate typing diagnostic takes 406 ms. The module still has 951 functions
  and 667 constants; `extractedProgram` has no axioms. Successful contracts
  use only standard Lean logical axioms. The native connected gate was not
  repeated for these proof-only changes.
- `mutations.log`: four admitted-but-wrong implementations fail their public
  contracts with exit 1 and no proof artifact: wrong subtree index, wrong
  sibling candidate, acceptance of the parent's end boundary, and wrong
  returned sibling. The batch takes 84.59 seconds and uses separate freshly
  admitted modules, not stale proof artifacts.
- `shared-final.log`: focused pure-statement, aggregate-expression, and call

## September 29: Shared matching and IR classification

The next structural layer now has actual-code contracts, not just example
executions. `IRShapeProof.actualExpected` covers all eight child rules and
every child position for a nonnegative i32-sized arity, including zero.
`actualNode` proves the shape-record constructor and is reused at its call
sites. `actualArm` handles arbitrary semantic i32 counts and ordinals: negative
counts, invalid ordinals, and wildcard bindings return an invalid shape;
successful arity calculation follows i32 wrapping. These calls terminate and
preserve the caller's existing cells, lexical scope, heap, world, and views.
Fresh parameter and pattern-binding cells are permitted.

The real `shape` dispatcher is covered for 62 of its 64 event constructors:
45 fixed-arity cases, 13 cases whose payload does not affect structure, three
record-payload counts, and the arm-header case. The ignored payload remains
arbitrary; counted records supply the actual fields, not an assumed successful
field read. Each family uses one small table-driven proof. A separate theorem
proves rejection of an arm header with no payload. Classification does not
claim to validate the types or contents of every payload: that is a different
obligation from choosing child structure.

The cast and match-expression header cases still depend on unproved
type-reference helper contracts. The tree constructor/validator's loop
invariants remain open, so `EndTable` bounds and storage facts are still caller
assumptions. This checkpoint does not prove those bounds, whole-tree printing,
source-to-IR/Surface/Core correspondence, frontend/lowering correctness,
x86/runtime refinement, verified self-hosting, or the trusted-output boundary.
The persistent goal remains active.

Shared `RunsExpr.matchValue` follows the existing pattern matcher, preserves
scrutinee effects, chooses the first matching arm, and restores caller scope.
Its pure version composes fresh local frames. Registered pure-call rules are
explicitly proved contracts; dispatch neither assumes calls are pure nor
reinterprets their bodies. `source_value` reads a constructor's existing
nominal metadata and proposes an ordinary Core value. It does not build
typing evidence, validate payload types, or alter the trust boundary; actual
execution contracts still check that representation. The ordinary emitted
Lean, production Lanius, and GPU compiler are unchanged.

Profiling exposed repeated proof attempts for skipped early-return guards.
A shared fallthrough/return rule checks the guard once on the successful
path. Closed enum comparisons now use the closed scalar path even when the
expected result is fixed; wrong results remain rejected by the kernel.
Symbolic Boolean conjunction/negation reuses the existing semantic rules.
These changes made the original classifier proof cheaper before scaling to
the event families. They do not establish a faster whole extraction pipeline.

Focused regressions cover arbitrary enum payloads, first-arm priority,
shadowed caller locals, scrutinee mutation, skipped traps, wrong enum IDs,
wrong payload length, wrong return values, false guards, and true branches
that still fall through. Metadata notation has record/enum and wrong-ordinal
checks, including deliberately malformed payloads for rejection proofs.
The event table initially assigned the wrong child rule to indexed places;
the actual-code proof rejected it. The model now requires a place followed
by an expression, matching indexed-place semantics.

## September 30: Complete IR classification

The classifier's two remaining event families now have connected contracts:
`IRShapeProof.actualCastEvent` and `actualMatchEvent`. They reuse proved
type-reference helpers and the existing shape constructor. Together with the
previous fixed, ignored-payload, counted, and arm-header families, this covers
all 64 event constructors. The contracts prove actual self-extracted calls,
their result, termination, and caller-frame preservation; successful execution
is not a premise. Invalid headers and type IDs are included, and successful
arity calculation follows the actual i32 wrapping behavior.

`IRTypeRefProof` proves six source-helper contracts: nominal code,
nominal-slice code, invalid-tag detection, code projection, validity, and
equality. Nominal IDs outside the reserved range return -1; addition inside
the range is proved without assuming its result. Two one-line specializations
select the I32 and Usize constructors at fixed call sites. This avoids asking
unification to invert the native-to-Core encoder.

Shared pure rules now compose helper calls in initializers, unary/binary
expressions, short-circuit disjunctions, and early-return guards. Fresh call
cells are allowed; existing caller cells, locals, heap, world, and views remain
protected. A tactic goal-rotation bug previously crossed into a neighboring
obligation; rotation now stays inside its current proof goal.

The connected check exposed arithmetic-automation regressions. Unrestricted
reflexivity and simplification of an unknown Boolean result could respectively
expand a large numeral or choose a literal result too early. The retained rule
proposes the scalar operation's Boolean predicate, and the kernel checks its
equality. Guard normalization handles decision predicates before generic
unification. No recursion or heartbeat limits were increased. Focused checks
retain wrong-result, mutation-as-pure, skipped-trap, first-arm, and scope
regressions, and add billion-sized bounds and a nonnegative signed comparison.

classifier from 210 to 265; the new type-reference file has 109 lines. Those
four scopes add 360 lines. Scalar/guard and arithmetic changes are additional;

not an overall repository or whole-compiler ratio.

### Shared tree traversal

The subsequent source refactor moves the duplicated child loop from `build`
and `valid` into private `children_end`. Both callers retain their parent-shape
and range checks. The helper returns -1 for a wrong child category, a
non-advancing endpoint, or an endpoint beyond the region; the callers translate
that into their existing rejection result. Successful cursor calculation is
shared. In the validated range, the old validator's `child != arity` and the
shared helper's `child < arity` traverse the same bounded sequence.

`RunsStmt.whileCountOrReturn` supplies total loop composition with ordinary
completion/continue or an early return. Its client must prove the decreasing
invariant or the returned result at each iteration. A general countdown test
proves that initial counts below three reach zero and larger counts reject at
three, with heap/world/views preserved. The rule does not itself prove the
source child loop: the event reads, classification, mutable cursor/counter,
and their invariant remain obligations. The foundation adds 27 physical
lines and the regression adds 56 lines, separately from the classifier ratio.

### Source-loop obligation at the preceding checkpoint

Prove `children_end` once and use it in both outer loops. Its contract must
derive either rejection or the exact cursor from the region's represented
events and existing endpoint suffix, rather than assume a successful run or
validator acceptance. The inner invariant retains `index + 1 <= cursor <=
count`, the number of consumed/remaining children, their required categories,
and strict endpoint advances. Its remaining-child measure decreases by one.

The builder's outer reverse induction must establish the endpoint suffix
before each parent: entries strictly after `index` are initialized and describe
their complete subtrees. Storing the newly computed endpoint extends that
suffix. Allocation, physical read/write bounds, i32 range bounds, caller frame,
and allocation-failure rejection belong in the actual-code contract. Only
after this is discharged can navigation stop assuming endpoint bounds/storage.

### Builder storage contracts closed (September 30)

`direct_ir_allocation.lean` closes three actual-code boundaries needed by
`ir::body_tree::build`, without assuming a successful allocation, constructor,
assignment, or validator run:

- `actualAllocate` proves the self-extracted `verified::host::alloc_i32` call
  for every natural word count through 536870911, including insufficient
  allocation budget. `actualReject` covers negative and oversized counts.
  The host call records its trace even on exhaustion; rejection does not call
  the host. Both outcomes preserve the caller's original cells and scope.
- `actualSlice` proves the source raw-slice wrapper from the fresh allocation
  metadata. The shared `allocatedI32_protect` rule handles the same builtin
  directly in the builder, where intrinsic lowering removes the wrapper call.
  Protecting the allocation changes ownership without copying its bytes,
  changing its frontier, or restoring its consumed budget.
- `actualEndpointStore` proves the builder's actual `ends[index] = cursor`
  expression. Readability and unique block identities imply successful store;
  they are not assumed execution results. The result stores the representable
  endpoint, preserves table readability, and retains all different entries,
  including the already-completed suffix. Its byte-write effect also describes
  all other addresses and unchanged allocation metadata.

The legitimate preconditions are well-formed caller cells, no registered
cell-backed views at the host/store boundary, existing allocation metadata,
old blocks below the frontier, unique block bases, represented local values,
readable endpoint storage, and index/value bounds. The whole builder still
has to derive its internal preconditions and compose these contracts with
`actualChildrenCorrect`. The results use the formal host/memory model; native
allocator/runtime refinement remains open. No typing tree, integer certificate,
normal-output change, native computation shortcut, or extra axiom was added.

Shared rules derive zero-filled fresh storage, old-read preservation,
ownership protection, loaded bytes from readability, exact written-byte reads,
and indexed assignment from a proved right-hand expression. Cast and bounded
unsigned multiplication contracts justify the actual widened allocation-size
calculation. The statement/field selectors select syntax only; proving a
selected expression still checks that it has the actual assignment shape.

#### Empty-slice semantic mismatch fixed

While connecting rejection to `empty()`, inspection found that the source
constructs its empty endpoint slice as `(null, 0)`, but `mapRawI32Slice`
required allocation metadata even at length zero. That trapped where the
source/native descriptor construction returns an empty slice. Nominal empty
slices already avoided backing-memory access.

The i32 interpreter now handles zero length in the same way: return the empty
descriptor and unchanged state before looking up or protecting backing storage.
Negative lengths still trap, nonempty slices still require valid allocation
metadata, and indexing an empty slice still fails bounds checking. The block
constructor lemma now explicitly requires positive length; its internal callers
migrate directly. No compatibility shim or stale alias was added.

`actualEmptySlice` proves the actual source raw-slice-wrapper call for any
pointer and well-formed caller cells, without heap or allocation assumptions.
The shared empty-descriptor expression rule composes proved pointer and length
evaluations; it does not assume a successful source call. Lean interpreter
regressions additionally run the actual `empty()` and zero-count builder calls
against the self-module and check their complete empty-tree representation.
Those two checks are tests, not proofs of the complete tree function.

- `empty-slice-shared-final.log` passes the raw-i32 and nominal-slice checks.
- `empty-slice-automation-final.log` passes the expression rules, raw-slice
  tests, and pure/call dependencies (35 jobs), including a negative proof-search
  test that rejects treating a nonempty null slice as empty.
- `empty-slice-contracts-final.log` passes all five actual contracts in 3.59
  seconds; `empty-slice-fixtures-final.log` passes the physical heaps and source
  empty-tree calls in 1.41 seconds. These reuse admitted, unchanged program data
  and rebuilt shared infrastructure. All audits retain only standard logical
  axioms. The fresh extraction timings above precede this interpreter fix.
- `empty-slice-byte-regression-final.log` rechecks the actual self-module byte
  writer's success/rejection contracts, including the migrated constructor
  setup, in 6.55 seconds. Its source proofs are mechanically reused with the
  self-module import, as in the existing self-check harness.
- Each mutant's `contract-after-empty-slice-final.log` rechecks the final
  contracts against already-admitted, unchanged mutant data. All three still
  fail their intended contract without a proof artifact or resource failure;
  the new empty-slice contract itself passes for every mutant.

the caller's cells and lexical scope.

native reads/classification, a canonical i32 active count, and existing heap
blocks below the allocation frontier. The caller must have well-formed cells,
unique heap bases, and no array views. These are explicit representation and
environment obligations, not assumed successful runs. The proof uses the
formal allocator/runtime model; native refinement and frontend establishment
of these facts are not claimed.

The new shared `RunsExpr.callPost` and `RunsStmt.letLocalPost` rules compose
fresh-cell framing and a proved postcondition across effectful call/local
scopes. Clients prove that their postcondition survives scope restoration;
these rules do not assume purity or erase heap/world effects. A focused
regression borrows owned storage and proves that the call retains the changed
ownership. The existing table contract now transports through pure returns
and lexical restoration. The loop's rejection result also retains the bad
index's bound, so callers do not reconstruct that fact.

execution nor prior validation is a premise. `Ready` is a logical contract,
not an emitted syntax tree, typing proof, buffer, or certificate.

`nextChild` proves the actual sibling call returns the next child or -1 after
the last one. Its frame preserves heap, world, and caller cells.

A one-line shared automation change lets a call reuse a local pure contract

## Built IR survives boolean leaf emission

The next proof boundary is an actual expression leaf: `surface_atom_output`
reads an `ExpressionBoolean` event from the built tree, selects its payload,
and calls `surface_boolean_output`. The checked helper emits `true` or `false`,
returns the next cursor, and frames all changes through the existing text
writer. A separate contract proves insufficient-space rejection with no output
write or borrowed string allocation. No successful printer run is a premise.

`ReadOnlyMemory` is a separated heap-fragment contract. It retains successful
reads from a reference heap whose readable addresses lie outside the output
buffer. `ofBlocks` establishes separation from allocation ranges; `afterOutput`
preserves the reads across checked writes and fresh borrowed string storage.
It reuses the existing recursive native-read theorem for structures, enums,
strings, and slice descriptors. This is a memory frame, not a second IR or
typing representation. Its inclusion and separation premises remain explicit;
the full frontend/driver proof must establish them.

`Ready` now stores actual event reads and endpoint reads, not preservation of
every byte in the builder's original heap. The builder's stronger postcondition
is unchanged and establishes those narrower facts. `Ready.afterOutput` and
`actualAtom_preservesIR` retain the same tree for the next consumer. This permits
an output buffer allocated before the IR, which an unchanged-whole-heap contract
would wrongly forbid. `RunsExpr.matchWriter` handles payload cells and caller
scope once, without reopening the proved writer branch.

Checks live under `target/verified-compiler/ir-shape/`:

- `read-only-tests.log`: kernel-checked inclusion/separation for data above an
  earlier output buffer, native/scalar read preservation, and refusal of an
  overlapping output buffer. The shared frame audits use only standard logical
  axioms.
- `surface-boolean-fresh.log`: the focused gate self-extracts afresh, admits that
  module, and rechecks writer and boolean contracts against it. The IR leaf and
  retention contracts may reuse builder dependencies only for byte-identical
  program data and non-stale direct proof sources. The gate checks those
  prerequisites before expensive work, and never reuses them for mutated data.
  The final complete gate passes in 69.93 seconds; its boolean contracts take
  6.32, IR leaf/retention contracts 3.49, and execution fixtures 1.13. An earlier
  run caught a stale navigation artifact after admission/writer work; the cache
  preflight now catches that condition before those expensive steps.
- `surface-boolean-mutation.log`: a `true` branch changed to emit `false` still
  admits as data, but fails `actualCall` without a proof artifact, heartbeat

- `surface-ir-final.log`: the actual IR boolean leaf and retention contracts

- `surface-builder-regressions.log`: refreshed navigation and builder-consumer

The Core-interpreter fixtures with native-layout memory cover both boolean values, exact output bytes and
cursor, tree navigation after printing, unchanged events/endpoints, and a

An attempted closed byte-level View proof hit reduction depth; it was removed
instead of raising limits or duplicating a layout/decoder proof per emitter.
The general printer contracts retain native View readability as an explicit
memory precondition.

## Scalar leaves reach the actual expression dispatcher

`direct_surface_ir.lean` now proves the atom's common native-read prefix and
selected writer branch once. The boolean and new integer atom proofs reuse
that contract in six proof lines each. The integer writer covers every
canonical i32, including minimum i32, using the existing signed formatter.

`direct_surface_dispatch.lean` proves the real expression dispatcher reaches
those leaves. Its public boolean and integer contracts discharge the classifier
and writer obligations; successful execution and classifier acceptance are not
premises. Boolean classification reads the existing annotation from native
memory, then calls the proved type-reference equality helper. It does not
reconstruct a typing derivation. Integer dispatch accepts nonnegative values
and minimum i32; other negative values belong in unary-negation IR nodes.

The shared writer rules now handle pure initializers, payload matches, returns,
and an early-return guard whose call can allocate fresh parameter cells.
`WriterProof` automates their composition. `PureProof` also handles nested calls
in argument lists, preserving caller reads after fresh-cell allocation. Focused
tests cover shadowing, nested calls, local reads after a call, wrong-result
rejection, and refusal to infer purity from an unframed successful run.

Assumptions remain explicit: the actual native View and event/annotation reads,
the represented builder tree, i32 and cursor/capacity bounds, well-formed cells,
empty legacy array views, and heap allocation invariants. Read preservation
after writes additionally needs the existing separated memory frame. Frontend
and driver proofs must establish these inputs. The Core fixtures are executable
regressions, not a proof of native initialization, x86 execution, or whole-printer
semantic preservation.

## Writer continuation boundary

`WriterEnvironment` packages the same four cell/heap assumptions already used
by the public scalar contracts. Checked pure scopes and writes derive the next
environment. No successful execution is stored in it. `Protected` names a
read-only heap fragment containing the native View; it excludes mutable output
storage. `Context.afterOutput` retains that native read and readable buffer while
advancing the cursor. These are memory contracts, not another IR or emitted
typing tree. Their separation/read assumptions still need driver establishment.

Uniform actual byte/text contracts now return `WriterFrame`. The boolean
helper's formatting proof uses them and shared guard automation rather than
handwritten call/local restoration. `RunsExpr.assignI32After` handles a writer's
effectful RHS followed by updating the resolved scalar local. It preserves the
RHS heap effects and requires its checked caller-cell frame, not an assumed
successful store. A kernel regression applies it to the real byte writer and
proves both output effect and updated cursor.

## First recursive writer: unary-not with a Boolean child

`direct_surface_unary.lean` proves the actual `surface_unary_output` helper for
a unary-not node whose child is either Boolean literal. It derives the node's
arity from `Classifies`, invokes the proved first-child navigator and actual
Boolean expression dispatcher, and derives every write and cursor assignment.
The result is exact bytes, the advanced cursor, termination, and `WriterFrame`.
Successful execution, classifier acceptance, or child-call correctness is not
an input assumption. Native View/event/annotation reads, a built tree, separated
input memory, room, and the usual writer environment are explicit prerequisites.
Frontend/driver proofs must still establish them.

`WriterCursor` anchors its cell frame before the cursor is bound. Its shared
advance rule retains the RHS writer effects and derives the store into that
fresh cell, without changing caller cells. Immutable local reads survive each
update. This is a local-state invariant, not a second syntax or typing tree.

The initial proof search reached the default heartbeat limit while matching
completed intermediate states. Scoping the finished write-chain proof inside
its body contract removes those states from the subsequent prefix search. The
final proof checks without raising resource limits; no new axiom or native
decision shortcut was added. Its audited axioms are the standard
`propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`.

## Unary-not through the normal expression dispatcher

`direct_surface_unary_dispatch.lean` proves `actualNotExpression`, the normal
`surface_expression_output` call for a unary-not parent with either Boolean
child. It proves the classifier returns false, the binary/intrinsic name
lookups return the empty name, and the actual match selects the unary helper.
The helper then derives the child dispatcher call and all writes. The result
is termination, exact bytes, next cursor, and the caller/input frame. The
contract retains the previous explicit native-memory, representation,
annotation, capacity, and writer-environment assumptions; it does not assume a
successful call or a correct route. All audited contracts use only `propext`,
`Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`.

`WriterCursor.advanceArguments` and `finishArguments` compose checked writers,
argument reads, cursor arithmetic, assignment, and final return. The real unary
helper uses both. `guardWriterFalse` and `letReadWriter` preserve read-only call
cells when execution continues into a writer. The dispatcher uses both, rather
than pretending its classifiers/name helpers leave the state identical.

Broad proof search initially reached the default recursion limit. Supplying
the known value-argument list and explicit retained native reads avoids that
search; a canonical event type at the read boundary also avoids needless
definitional matching through private aliases. No resource limit was raised.

## Closed recursion over unary-not chains

`actualNotStep` now accepts the proved child-writer continuation at the parent
boundary. `actualNotDispatch` handles the normal entry route independently of
the child expression. `Writer` quantifies over physical contexts with the same
represented tree, protected native input reads, available output capacity, and
the existing writer environment. It is a proof contract, not another IR or
emitted typing tree. The earlier `actualNotBoolean` and `actualNotExpression`
results discharge those continuations with the checked Boolean leaf.

`direct_surface_recursive.lean` closes the recursion by induction on chain
depth. `actualChainWriter` covers every finite chain whose existing region
contains unary-not parent events and a Boolean leaf with its native Bool
annotation. The builder's `Ready` view and classification facts establish
navigation; protected reads retain the tree across writes. The final theorem
does not assume child execution, successful output, or classifier acceptance.
It proves total printing, exact bytes, cursor advancement, and the writer
frame under the explicit environmental/capacity conditions in `Writer`.
The chain depth and byte-list model are proof specifications only; the
extractor still consumes its one physical typed IR and emits readable Lean.

program, producer/runtime/x86 proofs, verified self-hosting, and the explicit

## Closed Boolean-expression trees

`SurfaceBooleanTreeProof.actualTreeWriter` now closes structural induction for
arbitrary finite trees of Boolean literals, unary-not, logical-and, and
logical-or. `Represents` describes existing region events, physical child
endpoints, and native Bool leaf annotations. It is a proof specification, not
another generated tree, typing derivation, certificate, or serialized format.
The final contract quantifies over preserved native inputs, writable output
capacity, and the existing writer environment. It derives recursive calls and
proves termination, exact output bytes, cursor advancement, and the caller/input
frame. No successful run, dispatcher decision, or child-correctness premise
remains. The predicate requires Bool leaf annotations, not parent annotation
checks that this helper never performs; those typing facts still belong to
the producer proof. This is not yet a proof of semantic correspondence for
emitted Lean programs or of native x86 refinement.

The binary helper proof checks the actual pointer guard, operator-name lookup,
navigation, both recursive writes, intervening byte/text writes, and mutable
cursor stores. The pointer guard navigates both children even when its logical
operation flag is false. Child-chain evidence identifies the right child as
the left subtree's endpoint, not `index + 2`. Shared cursor rules support
read-only locals above the cursor and derive their preservation across cursor
updates. Lookup automation now tries reflexivity before `decide`: conditions
containing fresh cell variables can reduce directly without closed evaluation.
Existing name-helper proofs moved into the shared operations module. The
general tree induction replaces the unused 53-line chain-only module; the
existing unary-depth fixtures now import the general proof directly.

## Shared cursor-relative primitive calls

The unary and binary helper contracts now reuse `OutputWriterProof.byteAt`
and `textAt`. `Buffer.afterWriter` derives a readable current buffer and its
advanced position from the existing exact output frame. The byte/text lemmas
invoke the actual primitive contracts, normalize the frame address and returned
position, and derive the current environment from the prior frame. They require
available output capacity, not successful execution. Child navigation, recursive
writer calls, operator checks, and read-only local preservation remain explicit.

`WriterCursor.value` is registered with local lookup automation. This uses an
existing proved field; it introduces no new assumption. Callers no longer need
to copy that field into a separate fact before every write or rebuild a native
View context merely to call a primitive. Native contexts are still constructed
where a recursive expression writer actually reads the View and protected IR.

## One binary proof for all eighteen operators

`SurfaceExpressionTreeProof.actualTreeWriter` extends the closed printing domain
to i32 literals and all 18 binary operators, while retaining Boolean literals
and arbitrary unary-not nesting. It connects the existing physical tree and
annotation reads to the actual normal expression entry point. Structural
induction discharges both child-writer contracts; no successful execution,
correct output, dispatcher result, or recursive correctness is assumed.

The specification permits canonical nonnegative i32 literals and minimum i32,
matching the existing atom classifier; other negative literals use unary
negation, which is still outside this theorem. Boolean leaves retain their
existing native Bool annotation requirement. For `+`/`-`, `NonPointerInput`
requires an existing left annotation with I32, Bool, or Usize reference, not
the result of running the pointer guard. The proof reads that annotation via
the actual `result_type` helper and proves the pointer comparison false. The
other 16 operators short-circuit on their operation flag and need no new
annotation facts. No parent-annotation requirement was added to old Boolean
trees. Operand typing and arithmetic evaluation are not extra obligations of
this formatting theorem; the checked-IR producer and semantic correspondence
proofs remain separate and unfinished.

`actualBinaryStep` parameterizes the existing helper by operator spelling and
length. Its bounds argument covers one-, two-, and three-byte symbols, using
the same child navigation, cursor stores, and memory frame. `actualSymbol` and
the non-atom classifier prove the selected arm once, with only finite arm
selection split by operator. This avoids copying the complete execution proof
18 times. Shared read-only conjunction rules preserve nested call effects and
skip the remaining pointer tests after a false comparison.

The expression-tree proof and fixtures replace the Boolean-only files with
their actual contents moved and generalized. In-repo imports and the focused
gate use the new paths directly; no import-only shim or duplicate legacy proof
remains. The extractable IR, emitted format, compiler source, and bootstrap
executable are unchanged. These expression specifications are proof models,
not a second emitted syntax or typing tree.

at arbitrary finite depth. Its induction derives each child-writer contract
and invokes the actual shared unary helper and normal expression dispatcher.
Negative operands can therefore occur inside binary trees, not just as the
minimum-i32 literal accepted by the atom classifier. This is a printing
contract, not a proof of operand typing or arithmetic evaluation.

selection splits by operator. `actualUnaryDispatch` does the same for routing.

and binary routes. Existing Boolean-only helper/dispatcher corollaries were
unused and have been removed; their guarantees follow from the closed tree
theorem, with a checked example retaining that implication. Callers use the
new interfaces directly, without legacy aliases or import-only shims.

## Shared catalog-to-body name emission

The actual name path now has connected execution contracts, rather than an
assumption that lookup or copying succeeds:

- `SymbolSpellingProof.actualSpan` reads the native unit and token row, checks
  their bounds, and derives the selected source offset and length.
- `SourceIdentifierProof.actualCall` runs the actual byte-copy loop. Its loop
  invariant uses the existing two-local and read-only-memory infrastructure;
  each iteration invokes the proved byte writer and updates the real cursor
  and input-index cells.
- `EscapedIdentifierProof.actualCall` composes that copy with the real opening
  and closing UTF-8 delimiter writes.
- `SurfaceNameProof.actualReference` and `actualName` read the actual native
  View, select its catalog and unit, and invoke the shared escaped writer.

Successful contracts establish termination, exact bytes, cursor advancement,
and the caller/input frame. Their input specification records native catalog
reads, source spans, and output separation/capacity. It is a logical input
contract, not another emitted tree or typing proof. The producer still needs
to establish those representation facts. Unused catalog metadata is opaque;
these theorems do not assert that a partial naming catalog is a valid whole
program. Source copying is shared by later name consumers instead of being
reproved for each one.

Invalid unit references are proved to reject in actual catalog lookup and the
unescaped public helper before reading backing storage or accessing output.
Other invalid token kinds, spans, and ranges are tested, not universally proved.
The escaped helper may already have written its opening delimiter when an
invalid reference is rejected; no rollback guarantee is claimed. Body
`surface_reference` uses an explicit reference independently of the View's
current unit, whereas `surface_name` rejects a negative current unit.

  `surface-name.log`: both native-View consumer contracts check in 4.95 seconds.
  Audited transitive axioms remain only `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and

- `identifier-native.log`: all 76 focused native-layout Core-interpreter cases
