## Named locals in the closed recursive printer

`SurfaceLocalProof.actualExpression` now connects a typed `ExpressionLocal`
event to the normal expression entry point. It proves both actual payload
helpers, the atom classifier, the selected atom branch, and the dispatcher,
then invokes the shared body-name contract. No successful lookup, classification,
printing, or recursive execution is an input assumption.

The local contract records the existing native symbol reads and source span
through `SourceIdentifierProof.Name`. It assumes that the View selects that
catalog and unit, with output memory separated from input and enough space.
It does not claim that the name resolves to the right lexical binding or that
the printed Lean has the source program's semantics. Those producer and semantic
correspondence obligations remain open.

`SurfaceExpressionTreeProof.actualTreeWriter` extends the closed induction to
named locals nested under every supported unary and binary operation. The
existing scalar-only theorem remains unrestricted in its catalog context.
Trees with locals fix the View's immutable types/names/functions metadata,
function index, and unit. Parent steps carry that scope definitionally through
navigation and output writes, without another catalog read or proof of the copy
loop. Fixing all immutable metadata, rather than only names, leaves the same
interface usable for constructor and call proofs. This is a logical input
contract for the existing View, not a second emitted IR or typing tree.

The shared `RunsExpr.writerFramedArguments` rule composes read-only argument
calls with writers while retaining fresh parameter cells. The local-ID helper
uses that route before name emission. A checked nested-call regression proves
that caller scope is restored and both calls' frames are retained. This avoids
incorrectly treating read-only helper calls as state-identical evaluation.

## Constructor-name lookup and emission

`ConstructorNameProof.actualSlot` proves the actual `variant_slot` execution
from native declaration reads and bounds, independently of naming.
`actualIdentifier`, `actualSurface`, and `actualBody` connect the selected global
slot to its native catalog Ref, the existing escaped-name writer, and the actual
View wrapper. They derive execution, termination, exact bytes, cursor movement,
and caller/input framing. They do not assume successful lookup or writing.
`rejectsType` and `rejectsSurfaceType` prove out-of-range type rejection before
backing storage or output is accessed. The latter needs no valid output, naming
descriptor, or cursor assumption.

Success requires a named catalog, bounded type/variant metadata, the stated
native declaration/Ref/source-span reads, separated output, enough capacity,
and the existing writer environment. This does not establish name ownership,
lexical validity, producer facts, or the semantics of the resulting Lean.
Synthetic catalogs and general malformed-input rejection remain outside these
success/rejection contracts. No Lanius source, emitted format, typing tree,
trust policy, or resource limit changed.

The wrong-slot mutation initially exhausted the default heartbeat limit because
proof search retried already-proved prefixes after the wrong return failed.
`core_pure_node` now commits an initializer or returning guard before proving
its continuation. Its early-return case checks that the true branch really is
a return; ordinary fallthrough remains supported. Shared regressions cover
framed-state matching, rollback, nested guards/locals with both correct and
incorrect results, and the existing pure/effectful call cases. Contract-filter
experiments were discarded; the shared production change is 12 lines.

## Qualified declaration names and shared binding facts

`DeclarationNameProof.actualCall` now proves the actual Lanius
`declaration_identifier` call, including native module-path reads, each source
segment, separators, namespace selection, and the final escaped name. The
contract derives termination, exact bytes, cursor advancement, and caller/input
framing. `rejectsUnit` derives rejection before backing/output access for negative
or out-of-range source-unit IDs, without valid output or cursor assumptions.

The success domain supplies native source/token/unit/segment reads, consistent
catalogs, a nonempty path bounded by its unit's token count, disjoint output,
capacity, and the existing writer environment. It does not supply successful
writer execution. It does not yet derive lexical validity, name ownership,
these producer facts, or the semantics of the resulting Lean. Synthetic naming,
general malformed-input rejection, and the `type_identifier` wrapper remain open.

The source-name copy loop and qualified path loop share `WriterCounter`. Its
cursor updates retain the counter through nested calls; incrementing the fresh
counter retains the output and caller frame. The existing source-copy proof

lookup is checked once and reused within the same connected gate, not moved
outside its timing. The qualified proof separates native metadata preparation
from output composition; all phases still check in one invocation under default
resource limits.

arm; `actualBody` proves the native View-based `type_name` call. `rejectsIndex`

and writer environment—not successful emitter execution. Synthetic zero-unit
names, other type-reference arms, lexical/name validity, producer facts, and
semantic correspondence of the emitted Lean are outside these contracts.

equality; local-cell allocation does not invalidate a read. Tests cover wrong
heap/program/type/index, guessed-index/value rollback, local bindings, and

facts from qualified-name preparation; its complete module shrinks 395→357 lines.

closed recursive expression printer. For ordinary magnitudes below 2147483648,
the source digits determine the exact emitted expression text. No successful
decoder, lowerer, builder or recursive printer call is assumed.

from readable source/token storage and writable IR slots, requires enough
allocation budget, and ends in the printer's ordinary protected input/output
contract. Printer-environment construction, lexer/token provenance, notation
semantics, recursive lowering and the other compiler obligations remain open.
The signed minimum and resource failures are exercised in the connected runtime
fixture; their existing lowering proofs are not a source-to-printer proof.

The connection adds 172 proof/model lines and a 93-line fixture, reusing the
existing decoder, storage, builder and printer proofs. A shared native-write
lemma preserves heap-frontier validity for allocation. Fresh checking also
found a scope-composition failure in the existing byte writer; using the
existing forward-composition tactic fixed it without weakening the contract.

## October 1: arm structure and malformed-arm rejection

neither execution outcome is assumed. It retains the caller's cell prefix.

explicit. It is not general malformed-program rejection or a full heap/runtime
framing theorem for rejected inputs.

The shared `i32_succ_bound` lemma proves the count-plus-body increment cannot
overflow when its wrapped result is nonnegative and the input is already
bounded. Its 11 lines replace a stronger resource-bound assumption, not a
resource-limit increase. Kernel-checked adjacent boundary examples pass.
The existing `Input.fits` assumption still supplies the original count bound;
the caller/producer must eventually derive it and the initial local state.

## October 1: enclosing match function and normal expression entry

The next two match-emission obligations are closed. `SurfaceMatchProof.actualCall`
proves execution of the actual `surface_match_output` function from a normal
four-argument call. It derives the native View/event/header reads, allocates its
own cursor/child/ordinal locals, emits optional outer parentheses and the
scrutinee parentheses, invokes the closed finite-expression and complete arm-loop
proofs, returns the exact cursor, and restores caller scope. Its `WriterFrame`
covers exact output bytes, old caller cells, input storage and runtime state.
`actualExpression` connects this result to the actual ordinary expression entry
and its literal fourth argument `true`.

The domain is explicit native-data representation, not successful execution.
`Match` relates an existing builder tree and catalogs to a finite scrutinee and
arm list. Scrutinee and arm bodies use Boolean/i32/local/unary/binary expressions.
Named/wildcard patterns use the existing catalog/name relations. The call still
requires a well-formed writer environment, separated/readable input, matching
metadata, capacity, and signed-count bounds. The builder's accepted match shape
supplies the positive arity and child chain; callers no longer supply live outer
controls. These are formatting/execution contracts, not a proof that the producer
establishes every premise or that the emitted Lean preserves source semantics.

Three shared changes reduce repeated work: execution contracts can be compared
at a common proved fuel (`RunsExpr.agree`); cursors retain lexical slot facts
across reader/writer calls (`slotsEq`); and optional writer branches need a
successful writer contract only when their guard is true (`branchArguments`).
The two operand delimiters share one actual-reader/byte-writer proof. The shared
fallback dispatch rule now supports literal trailing arguments. Unary and
constructor callers explicitly supply none; the match route supplies `true`.
There is no additional emitted tree, certificate, transport, or trust assumption.

## October 1: derive the builder's complete physical table

The native builder already proved its later suffix valid when rejecting the
current node, but `StructuralResult` dropped that fact. Retaining it lets the
shared boundary discharge rejection for valid source structure. The builder
executes once; the proof carries its facts forward instead of validating,
copying, decoding or reconstructing the physical endpoint table.

`ChildChain.endpointUnique` and `Source.equalEndpoints` show that the preorder
shape uniquely determines every in-range subtree end. `ChildChain.congrEnds`
now requires agreement only inside the actual input range. `Source.noBadNode`
then excludes a bad next node when the real reverse loop has established the
later suffix. The canonical endpoints here are a mathematical specification,
not a second generated tree or a prepared native endpoint table.

`IRBuilderNavigationProof.actualCompleteCall` composes these facts with the
actual full builder call. Under independently stated well-shaped source data,
single-root, positive-count, capacity, representable-size and allocator-budget
facts, it produces a `Ready` view whose endpoints equal the canonical ones at
every valid index. It proves termination and caller-cell framing; event reads
come from the builder's actual old-heap read guarantee. Well-formed caller cells,
unique heap block bases and no legacy i32-array views remain explicit model
assumptions. Bounds, allocator failure and malformed-structure results remain
covered by the existing total `actualCall` contract; they are not silently
discarded from that theorem.

The concrete leaf's native-storage example now supplies its simple shape facts
and invokes this generic theorem. It no longer redoes allocation-failure, bad-
node and root-rejection cases. The fixture module shrinks 214 to 205 physical
lines while retaining its whole-call and immediate physical-traversal claims.
Existing executable fixtures cover parent/leaf trees, spare capacity, forests,
missing children, wrong child categories, allocation exhaustion and navigation.

`ir_producer_check.sh` checks all eight builder-related contracts and fixtures
in one fresh artifact. It first compares the current source closure and fresh
self-output with the byte-identical admitted program. That admission is reused;
no cached builder contracts substitute for the changed proofs. The final check
takes 43.66 seconds, and the whole command takes 51.44 seconds. Evidence:
`target/verified-compiler/ir-shape/ir-producer.log`. Successful audits list only
`propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`.

This is a correctness step, not a checking-speed improvement or a compactness

## October 1: typed append derives its physical writes

The next producer boundary is the actual `ir::body_events::append` call, not
another validator for the resulting IR. `IREventStoreProof.actualAppend` now
proves it on annotated regions: the supplied event is stored, the annotation
becomes `Invalid`, the result is `position + 1`, and the caller cells/world,
heap allocation metadata and all memory outside the two slots are preserved.
`actualReject` proves an out-of-range position returns -1 before either store,
even when the descriptors are null and the heap is empty.

The common rules are in `Semantics/NativeEncoding.lean` and
`Semantics/NativeStore.lean`. Little-endian encoding round-trips at its declared
width under an explicit representability bound. `NativeStoreSpec` derives the
real native store and read-back from readable storage and unique block bases.
Primitive i32/Boolean and empty/single-payload enum rules compose; each enum
uses its actual declaration, ordinal and payload types. `NativeSnapshot`
retains the value justified by its slot's bytes. Its disjoint-write rule lets
the annotation reset preserve the newly stored event without reopening the
event's decoder. These are logical memory contracts, not runtime records, an
emitted proof tree, or a new intermediate output format.

`RawStore.lean` now shares place resolution and assignment between i32 and
nominal stores. Its typed rule derives execution, the snapshot and the heap
frame from a proved codec; the existing scalar rule retains its exact byte
effect. `core_lookup` also handles closed structure and enum lookup, with the
same kernel-checked reflexivity proof used for functions and constants.

The append contract still needs a represented current event, an annotation
snapshot from initialization or an earlier write, in-range native indices,
writable separated slots, well-formed caller cells, unique heap block bases
and no registered legacy i32-array views. These are legitimate input/memory
facts, not successful append or native-write premises. Concrete codecs close
arbitrary signed i32, Boolean and null events. Structured/multi-field payload
codecs and the unannotated success path remain open; so does the actual
`record_result_type` contract. Do not call this complete frontend lowering.

The fixture uses real storage with nonzero padding and a previous `Void`
annotation. A kernel theorem covers every signed i32 word and proves both
neighboring event/annotation slots unchanged. Core-evaluator regressions cover
zero, seven, minus one, signed extremes, both booleans, null events, and both
positions. Bounds rejection is also witnessed with empty storage. These checks
do not establish x86 machine-code refinement or verified executable provenance.

`ir_producer_check.sh <extractor> events` checks this connected component in
one fresh artifact; the default checks it together with every existing builder
contract and fixture. Both modes compare current source closure and fresh
self-output against the unchanged admitted program before reusing admission.
The final event artifact takes 4.62 seconds; its whole gate takes 12.03 seconds.
The final default artifact takes 45.30 seconds, and its whole gate takes 53.28
seconds. All accepted audits list only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and
`Quot.sound`. Evidence: `target/verified-compiler/ir-producer/event-producer.log`
and `target/verified-compiler/ir-shape/ir-producer.log`. Earlier failed fixture
checks are not accepted artifacts; the final default rerun includes the fixes.

## October 1: close result-type recording and reuse forward composition

`IRResultTypeProof.actualRecord` now proves the real self-emitted
`ir::body_events::record_result_type` call, not a model or validator of its
result. Given an in-range, writable, represented annotation slot, a valid
TypeRef, well-formed caller cells, unique heap block bases and no legacy array
views, it returns true and stores that exact native type. A `NativeSnapshot`
proves read-back, while `HeapRegionWrite` preserves every byte outside the

views are also retained. No event-buffer storage or successful-store premise
is required. The codec derives the native store for every actual TypeRef
constructor, including both nominal payload variants.

`actualOutOfBounds`, `actualInvalid` and `actualDisabled` close the other
paths with read-only frames. Bounds rejection occurs before type validation
or buffer access. Invalid type codes reject before storage access. Annotations
disabled still validates the type but needs no memory; Void is accepted as a
function result. `IRTypeRefProof.actualValidResult` proves the real validation
helper using the existing code-helper contract.

`core_stmt_forward` follows actual statement order and composes proved
expression outputs before choosing the continuation. It does not execute a
checker or program and does not synthesize heap effects. A branch condition
is proved once; a later branch failure does not retry its prefix under another
branch. Both real store proofs now use this shared rule. The append module
is 129 lines instead of 143; its statement composition is one tactic rather
than a manual trace. Shared tests prove consecutive-effect composition,
early return and local-scope restoration, and check that an arbitrary write
without an execution contract is refused. The shared test build passes.

The annotation fixture uses two real slots with nonzero padding. Its kernel
theorem covers any valid TypeRef and preserves the neighbor byte-for-byte.
It deliberately supplies an unusable event descriptor, which the function
never reads. Empty-heap examples cover out-of-bounds rejection and disabled
Void success. The 144 Core-evaluator cases cover 18 types/payloads, four
positions and both enable modes, including valid/invalid nominal-ID boundaries

## October 1: close actual region construction and connect its writers

`IRRegionProof.actualRegion` proves the real `ir::body_events::from_raw_parts`
constructor on its fresh-storage domain. `FreshRegion` states positive capacity,
signed byte-count and pointer bounds, aligned backing allocation, both subranges
in that allocation, and zero annotation tags. It assumes neither successful
construction nor valid IR. The actual initialization loop visits every slot,
executes its Invalid store and increment, and terminates at capacity. Both slice
descriptors have the correct backward native addresses and lengths. Borrowing
transfers ownership without copying or changing bytes; caller cells, world and
views are retained. Arbitrary reused storage with nonzero tags is outside this
theorem. The real allocator/caller must still establish these facts generally;
Linux mmap and x86 runtime refinement are not proved by this checkpoint.

Shared `Memory/BorrowRange` proves read preservation, block lookup, unique bases,
idempotence and the actual range-borrow operation. `Memory/StoreSame` proves that
writing existing bytes retains the heap; the native empty-enum writer derives
the same result for zero tags. `RawSlice` constructs nominal slices from backing
allocation facts. Natural pointer arithmetic and `CountedLocal.increment` prove
the real operations without executing a per-program validator. Counter assignment
now shares one scoped rule with decrement; the existing decrement tests pass.

The complete body uses `core_stmt_forward`, including its supplied loop contract
and returned record. A failed attempt initially exhausted Lean's recursion limit
while expanding local bindings. The fix selects expression/argument/statement
relations and syntax before applying semantic rules, composes argument effects
forward, and tries aggregate construction before Boolean-guard search. Contract
lookup rejects different syntax before comparing states. No limits were raised.
Shared regressions cover state/syntax mismatch rollback, effects after loops,
returned records, lexical restoration, and rejection of unproved writes and
nonterminating loops. The fresh shared test build passes.

`IRRegionTests.constructAppendRecord` connects the three actual calls on a
nonempty owned allocation. Zero tags coexist with nonzero padding and valid
existing event payloads, so the domain is inhabited without assuming all bytes
are zero. The kernel theorem covers every signed i32 payload and valid TypeRef,
derives writer preconditions from constructor output and preceding writes,
preserves the final event through type recording, and retains neighboring bytes.
Core-evaluator checks cover capacities 1, 2, 3 and 8, every annotation, exact
descriptors, unchanged bytes and borrowed ownership. These supplement the proof;
they do not establish native x86 execution or trusted executable provenance.

The `regions` gate checks type helpers, stores, constructor, annotation recording

producer/builder artifact passes in 34.62 seconds; its whole gate takes 43.82
seconds, compared with the previous smaller artifact's 60.55-second check and
67.91-second whole gate. Shared infrastructure and admission only of the
byte-identical self-program are reused; both gates freshly emit and compare that
program and check current source closure. These are connected measurements, not
a controlled attribution benchmark. All successful audits use only `propext`,
`Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`. Evidence: `ir-producer/regions.log`,
`ir-producer/shared-tests.log` and `ir-producer/producer-current.log` under
`target/verified-compiler`.

Physical counts: region contracts/helpers 225 lines, constructor 18, fixtures
118. The roughly 12.5x program-specific ratio still misses the 5x target. New
shared borrow and same-store modules total 131 lines; other existing arithmetic,
loop, syntax-selection and automation modules also grew. Reuse does not erase
that initial cost. Avoid multiplying this call/memory setup across other producers.

Next: derive the fresh-region facts from its real allocator/caller, close the

## October 1: derive fresh storage and close allocation-to-writer composition

`IRRegionAllocationProof.actualOwned` closes the three real catalog initializers:
`allocation_bytes(event_count)`, `host.alloc(bytes, 8)`, and
`from_raw_parts(memory, event_count)`. The size/bytes binding is computed, not
assumed. Shared `Memory.Allocated` proves the actual allocation result, alignment,
positive base, zero-filled byte ranges, old-read preservation, unique bases and
the post-allocation frontier. Constructor inputs follow from that result. The
contract preserves caller cells, world/views and old memory while initializing
every annotation. `actualSizeReject` and `actualMemoryExhausted` cover size
rejection and null-on-exhaustion separately; they do not prove the enclosing
catalog guards/copy loop.

`InitializersRun` is a proof-only name for the three real execution relations
and the intervening local bindings. It replaces an exposed tuple of six states
and a dependent constructor-input record. It is not a certificate, emitted IR,
assumed successful trace, or a second typechecking tree.

`IRRegionPipelineProof.actualAllocateAppendRecord` composes those initializers
with actual append and type recording. Its domain covers any represented count,
any in-range slot, every signed i32 payload and every valid TypeRef. It derives
readability, zero initial event/annotation snapshots and slot separation from
the allocation; no successful write or supplied internal storage context replaces
an obligation. The result retains caller cells and all old readable memory and
supplies event/type snapshots. Shared native snapshot constructors, nominal-slot
address normalization and fresh-region framing carry those facts. Byte writes
reuse the same fresh-region framing rule rather than duplicate its proof.

The 140-line fixture includes two kernel theorems and executable cases. An old
nonzero canary survives allocation, construction and both writers. Seven size
cases exercise signed/count boundaries; twelve allocation cases exercise rounded
frontiers, exact budgets and exhaustion. There are 210 complete writer pipelines
over capacities 1/2/3/8, every position, five signed boundary payloads, and three
valid types. Each checks every event/annotation slot and unchanged old storage.
Shared allocator tests also cover zero-size base reservation and invalid alignment;
native-read/slice regressions and their axiom audits pass.

The final fresh connected producer/builder artifact passes in 37.98 seconds;
the whole gate takes 45.75 seconds. Only shared libraries and admission of the
byte-identical self-program are reused. The gate freshly emits and compares
self-output, checks current source closure and checks all current producer
contracts/fixtures together. Successful audits list only `propext`,
`Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`. Evidence under `target/verified-compiler`:
`ir-producer/producer-allocation-pipeline.log`,
`ir-producer/regions-allocation-pipeline.log`, and
`ir-producer/allocation-shared-tests.log`. Earlier failed diagnostic artifacts
are not accepted proofs.

Physical size accounting: new program-specific allocation/pipeline modules
213 + 114 = 327 lines; new fixtures 140; shared allocator and its tests 125 + 39.
The entire connected contract/helper scope (TypeRef, event store, result type,
region construction, region allocation and pipeline) is 921 lines. Its matched
Lanius scope is 91 lines: allocation size (4), constructor (17), append (10),
annotation recording (10), the three caller initializers (5), and seven TypeRef
helpers (45). Function counts include declaration through closing brace; module
counts include all imports, helper definitions, statements, comments and blanks.
This is about 10.1x, not the 5x target. Shared foundations, fixtures and generated
data remain additional costs. Previous constructor counts included a trailing
blank; this count uses a consistent function boundary. Do not grow this by
copying call/heap setup into each frontend proof.

Next: close remaining aggregate event layouts and connect the native frontend's
event producer to shape, annotations, catalog references and source meaning.
The full catalog append still needs its guards, duplicate scan, copy loop, tree
construction and row/count writes. These are Core-model contracts, not native
OS allocator/ELF/x86 refinement or bootstrap executable authentication. General
extractor preservation/rejection, verified self-hosting and the trusted-output
boundary remain open. No production source, emitted format, Rust/Python tool,
JSON, integer certificate, second typing tree, new axiom, native-computation
dependency or proof limit changed. The goal remains active and incomplete.

## October 1: close native storage for every Event schema

`IREventCodecProof.representedCodecs` now proves actual native write/read-back
and framing for all 64 current Event variants. The domain covers independently
arbitrary represented i32 fields, Boolean fields, every TypeRef, all three slice
kinds and bounded raw slice descriptors. Eleven record layouts cover casts,
locals/declarations, field references, struct/enum headers, match headers/arms,
calls, typed slices and strings. Nullary headers follow the actual declaration
metadata, not a hand-maintained opcode list. A kernel coverage check accounts for
every actual ordinal exactly once and fails when an uncovered variant is added.

Shared `NativeStoreSpec.record` proves the real field fold at arbitrary arity:
each field's proved codec, actual offset, slot bounds and field separation yield
successful stores, retained earlier fields, a whole-record snapshot and a frame
over all other bytes/allocation metadata. `native_store` reads actual metadata
to propose offsets/widths and then proves each premise in the kernel. It preserves
other tactic goals and supports registered proved codecs such as TypeRef and
SliceElementKind. Call/match proofs are `by native_store`, not copied heap proofs.
The generic raw-slice rule writes and reads exact 64-bit pointer/length words.
It does not touch or justify the referent. String storage is modeled explicitly,
not quantified over the empty surface-syntax Slice type.

`actualAllocateAppendRecord` accepts any proved native event codec.
`IRRegionAllocationTests.allocateAppendRepresented` discharges that obligation
for every represented schema, arbitrary count/position and valid annotation.
The proof starts from real count bindings, an exact budget and an old nonzero
canary; it executes actual allocation/construction, append and type recording,
and retains old memory. The prior scalar fixture is generalized, not copied.
These remain Core host-model contracts, not OS/native executable refinement.

512 executable append cases cover every variant at both slots over four mixed
field assignments, signed extremes, nested enum cases, maximum 64-bit descriptors,
annotation reset, nonzero padding and unchanged neighboring bytes. Kernel proofs
cover arbitrary fields/counts, not only those examples. Shared rejection tests
cover wrong arity/identity, insufficient width/fuel, unsupported slices and
truncated pointer/length words. Native-read framing regressions also pass.

The final fresh connected producer/builder artifact checks in 40.13 seconds;
the full gate takes 47.59 seconds. Shared libraries and admission of the
byte-identical self-program are reused; source closure, self-output and all
current producer contracts/fixtures are checked afresh. All successful axiom
audits list only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`. Evidence:
`target/verified-compiler/ir-producer/producer-aggregate.log` and
`aggregate-shared-tests.log`; earlier failed diagnostic artifacts are not proofs.

Size accounting: the new program-specific schema contract/helper module has
127 physical lines for 114 lines of matched payload declarations. Shared record,
slice and automation modules add 314 lines, plus four in NativeStore. Shared tests
add 46 lines; new/extended program fixtures add 90. The connected storage scope
(TypeRef, event/annotation stores, codecs, region construction/allocation/pipeline)
is now 1,050 lines, excluding shared foundations, tests and generated data.
The earlier function-only scope remains about 10x its implementation; adding
datatype coverage is not proof compression or a demonstrated 10x reduction.

Next: prove that actual frontend event production supplies the shapes,
annotations, catalog references, live referents and source meaning these stores
preserve. Complete catalog guards/copy/build/writes, general extraction
preservation/rejection, runtime/x86 refinement, verified self-hosting and the
trusted-output boundary remain open. No production Lanius source, emitted format,
Rust/Python tool, JSON, certificate, second typing tree, new axiom, native

## October 2: connect actual frontend writers to native storage

The next proof boundary is frontend production, not more event codecs.
`IREventWriteProof` now proves the actual `expression_value`, `expression_call`
and `expression_match` bodies. Literal dispatch/payload binding, guarded header
validation, exact event storage, annotation reset, cursor advance and memory
framing are connected. Bad call/match headers reject without storage premises.

`Produces` states the common slot contract. `MemoryEffect` and its proved scope
laws carry heap effects through parameter binding, pure guard evaluation,
pattern bindings, returns and scope restoration. Call automation specializes
applied rules before their positional arguments; `core_state` only selects
the current semantic input state. Neither executes a checker or invents effects.

The allocation pipeline is generalized in place to `actualAllocateProduceRecord`;
there is no second copied pipeline or old compatibility wrapper. Its closed
`allocateLiteralRecord` instance supplies the proved actual literal writer,
derives slot facts from allocation, installs its I32/Bool annotation, reads
back both values and preserves pre-existing memory for arbitrary counts/slots.
This is composition of real calls, not yet the complete `body_expression` caller.

The next level is also closed for Boolean literals: `actualBoolean` proves the
Boolean branch of `body_literal.emit`, for both values and every represented
token ID. Its context may lack source/token buffers because this path reads
only the parsed node operator. `allocateBooleanLoweringRecord` supplies that
actual caller to the allocation pipeline and records Bool, with read-back and
old-memory preservation. Other literal branches and recursion remain open.

Fresh connected producer/builder proof: 44.09 s; whole gate: 51.94 s. Shared
libraries and only byte-identical self-program admission are reused. Source
closure, self-output and all current selected proofs/fixtures are fresh.
All successful audits list only propext, Classical.choice and Quot.sound.
120 new writer/caller cases cover both slots, bounds rejection, invalid headers,
extreme signed words, nested types, exact payloads and neighboring byte frames.
Shared call/effect/writer regressions pass. Logs: `producer-frontend.log`,
`lowering-writer.log`, `scoped-effect-tests.log` in the ir-producer output folder.

Size: 107 writer contract/helper lines for 24 source-function lines, plus 50
Boolean-caller lines for its 9-line path (header, operator binding and Boolean
branch, not the whole function). Including net 22 store-interface lines, this
is about 5.4x. Shared effect rules add 104 lines;
call-automation changes and fixtures remain additional costs. This does not
compress the previous roughly 10x allocation proof scope or finish the goal.

Next: connect frontend callers to event shapes, catalog identities, typing,
annotations and recursive source meaning. A match writer alone does not check

## October 2: source bytes now reach the integer IR writer through proved calls

The connected path is `constant_expression.decimal` →
`body_context.integer_literal`/`decimal` → the integer branch of
`body_literal.emit` → the existing proved native event writer. It derives the
payload from source bytes selected by the actual token row, rather than assuming
a successful decoder call or supplying the desired number directly to a writer.
Malformed/overflowing contents reject without an output-storage premise.
The decoder separately rejects bad span bounds before reading memory.

Simplified the Lanius decoder to a typed accumulator and cursor. Its reference
model uses mathematical radix-10 append, independent of the implementation's
division guard. The existing separator behavior and early rejection are
preserved. The proof covers any readable in-range nonempty span, not just test
strings. Token provenance and lexical separator placement are not proved here.

Fresh connected producer/builder check: 61.98 s; whole gate: 69.41 s. New
self-module admission: 13.71 s. Only shared infrastructure and byte-identical
self admission are cached in the connected check. Eleven decoder cases, seven
source-to-IR cases and the existing producer fixtures pass. The native decoder
fixture passed both implementations, including 264 roundtrips and 130 single-word
inputs. All audited contracts use only standard logical axioms.

The requested Choice example also passes: emitted Lean admission 1.03 s;
termination/return-7/pure-frame proof 1.47 s; actual Lanius-produced x86 returns 7.
Both tools were freshly bootstrapped by the GPU compiler. The Choice proof is
now included in the existing direct-emission gate; that whole gate was not rerun.

The matched decoding/caller proof scope is 627 physical lines for 108 source
lines, about 5.8x, including helper models and the existing Boolean caller.
Shared rules add 65 lines plus separating blanks. Tests, generated output and
the reused writer/storage proofs are additional costs. The shared regression
checks aggregate assignment after a real call that shadows the caller's local,
then increments the second local without losing the aggregate or changing heap.

Use the explicit `extractor-decimal` executable and `decimal` admission cache
with `ir_producer_check.sh`; the old default tools/caches remain untouched.
All logs are under `target/verified-compiler/decimal`. No JSON, certificate,
second typing tree, new axiom, native proof evaluation or raised limit was added.

Next: compose the remaining literal and recursive frontend callers, rather than
adding more storage-codec proofs. Integer source decoding is now connected, but
the negative-literal caller, strings, full catalog append, general extraction
preservation/rejection, native x86/runtime refinement, verified self-hosting and
the explicit trusted-output boundary remain open. The goal remains incomplete.

## October 2: negative source literals reach their actual IR representation

`IRNegativeLoweringProof` connects the real token reader and decimal decoder
to `body_literal.negative`. Ordinary magnitudes produce a unary-negation node,
its positive integer child and the child's I32 annotation. The signed-minimum
magnitude produces one representable negative integer. Malformed contents
reject before any output access, even with an invalid output descriptor.
The proof retains the existing IR shape; no production Lanius changed here.

The source span must be readable, nonempty and in range. Success requires two
valid consecutive native slots for ordinary negatives, or one for the minimum,
plus the existing Core cell/heap invariants. The five-write relation frames
unrelated bytes and retains the final node snapshots. It does not assume that
the writers or annotation guard succeeded. Token provenance, bad caller spans
and insufficient-capacity/partial-write paths remain separate obligations.

Shared rules now handle an effectful call inside unary negation and a
short-circuit guard. Lookup automation derives preservation from both an
execution contract and a fresh-cell frame; a regression rejects the same
claim without the frame. This removed manual local transport and duplicated
call syntax. The negative module fell from 260 to 234 physical lines for a
21-line caller and six-line helper (~8.7x). The unary-writer contract and
disjoint-slot helper add 23 and 14 lines; the fixture adds 88. Existing
decoder/storage proofs and new shared rules/tests remain additional costs.
The size warning remains: do not replicate this manual storage composition
for every recursive expression case.

Final fresh connected producer/builder check: 74.55 s; whole gate: 81.96 s.
Shared infrastructure and admission of the byte-identical self-program are
reused; current self-output, source closure and selected proofs/fixtures are
checked afresh. Nine negative cases and a kernel source-to-two-node theorem
pass alongside the existing producer tests. Shared regressions pass in 7.50 s
including incremental builds. All audited theorems use only standard logical
axioms. Logs: `decimal/negative-final.log` and `decimal/negative-shared.log`.

The goal remains incomplete. Strings, recursive source meaning, catalog
correctness, general extraction preservation/rejection, native x86/runtime
refinement, verified self-hosting and the trusted-output boundary remain open.
